- 2026-05-18 · 2.9 chose sibling providers + pure-aggregator `useApp()` hook (no shim AppContext) over a single-Provider-with-internal-contexts split · The hook-aggregator path is the cleanest extensible end state: every screen that migrates off `useApp()` onto direct `useExpenses()` / `useCategories()` subscribes only to its slice and avoids the all-state re-render. The merged shim only exists for backwards-compat until screens migrate. Trade-off: shim consumers (every current screen) subscribe to ALL 9 contexts — same blast radius as today's single AppContext, no worse · Impact: zero screen edits in 2.9 itself; per-screen migration to direct hooks becomes a per-screen cleanup task. Each new screen written from scratch should import its slice hook directly, not `useApp()`.
- 2026-05-18 · 2.9 cross-feature edge: `pots` live in `ExpensesProvider`, not `CategoriesProvider` · The SQL summary query is `SELECT c.id, ..., SUM(e.amount) FROM categories LEFT JOIN expenses ... GROUP BY c.id` — primarily an expenses aggregate keyed on category. Putting pots in Categories would mean every expense mutation has to call into a hook outside its own provider; putting them in Expenses means category mutations call `useExpenses().refreshSummary` after their own DB write, which is the simpler dependency direction (Categories sits inside Expenses in the provider tree so it can read `useExpenses()`) · Impact: `useApp().pots` (and `useExpenses().pots`) both work and return identical data. CategoriesProvider returns just the `categories` rows; pots-as-derived-view is owned by the provider that owns the underlying summary state.
- 2026-05-18 · 2.9 introduced a small `RefreshBus` context (`core/state/RefreshBus.js`) instead of passing `refresh` callbacks down through props · `resetApp` and pull-to-refresh need to fan out across every slice; without a bus, each provider would have to expose its `refresh` upward through a prop or a deep callback chain. Ref-based registry sidesteps re-render churn (no state inside the bus) and lets providers self-register on mount via `useRegisterRefresh(key, fn)` — the entire pattern is ~30 lines · Impact: testable in isolation, mock-able for future tests, and the bus also doubles as the hook point for any cross-feature event (e.g. "user opened the app from background" maintenance triggers per 8.7).
- 2026-05-18 · 2.10 exposed read-only repo methods on the providers via arrow wrappers (`netWorth: (...a) => accRepo.netWorth(...a)`) instead of binding methods directly · `items.topMover()` internally calls `this.trackedItems()` — exposing `topMover: itemRepo.topMover` as a bare reference loses the `this` binding and crashes on call. Arrow wrappers preserve the receiver and cost nothing at runtime. Applied uniformly even for methods that don't use `this.` today, so future repo changes can't silently regress · Impact: pattern is consistent across all four exposed-method providers (items, accounts, travel, expenses); 2.10 callers can rely on the methods working regardless of internal repo style.
- 2026-05-18 · 2.10 dropped `useApp` import from `components/ItemRows.js` entirely · ItemRows only ever read `repos.items.suggest` from useApp; after migrating to `useItemActions().suggest`, the useApp call is dead weight. Removing the import (rather than leaving a stale destructure) keeps ItemRows decoupled from the legacy aggregator — a step closer to the day useApp is deleted · Impact: ItemRows now subscribes only to ItemsContext (which has no React state — its value object is built once with `useMemo`), so component re-renders only when its own props/state change. Template for the same pattern in screens once they migrate slice-by-slice.
- 2026-05-18 · 2.13 ScanService kept the save path in Scan.js, not the service · The roadmap line "Scan.js becomes a thin consumer" maps cleanly to extracting the OCR→parse→category-guess pipeline (the OCR-specific orchestration). The save flow is React-orchestration concern — needs `toast`, `navigation`, `setSavingExpense`, ImagePicker stage handling — none of which belong in a pure service that future batch importers or tests might call. Leaving save in Scan.js keeps the service genuinely portable: callable from a console, a test runner, or a future batch-receipt importer with no React surface · Impact: service is 57 LOC, zero React, zero side effects (except OCR via `recognize`). Scan.js dropped 19 LOC and a CATEGORY_MATCHERS map. Future "stitched multi-receipt" feature (4.24) can compose multiple `processReceipt` calls without touching the React layer.
- 2026-05-18 · 2.13 ScanService exposes both `processReceipt` (pure: OCR result → review payload) and `scanAndProcess` (async: uri → review payload) · The pure form is testable without mocking ML Kit — feed it a hand-built OCR object and assert the suggested category. The async form is what Scan.js actually calls today. Splitting the two surfaces the seam tests will need first (4.19 — OCR golden dataset) · Impact: when 4.19 lands, the test fixture pipeline becomes `loadGolden(name) → processReceipt(ocrFixture, potsFixture)` with no native module required.
- 2026-05-18 · 2.14 cache scope: module-level `Map` shared across all `useHomeDashboard()` consumers, not a `useRef`-per-mount cache · The user-selected option matches the 5.D `useQuery` pattern from task 8.9, so this hook doubles as the smallest precursor of that work — same cache shape, same TTL gate, same invalidation API. A `useRef` would have lost the data on every Home unmount (tab switch), defeating the 30 s window in the most common navigation path · Impact: tab-switch back to Home within 30 s now skips all 4 queries entirely (`netWorth`, `nextTrip`, `streakDays`, `topMover`). When 8.9 lands the cache logic generalises straight into the shared `useQuery` with no contract change for Home.
- 2026-05-18 · 2.14 cache eviction signal is `expenses[0]?.id` (top-of-list), not `expenses.length` or a full hash · Adding an expense or editing the most-recent one changes the head row's id; deleting the most-recent does the same (id of new head differs). For the rare back-dated insert that doesn't touch the head, the cache stays warm until the user pull-to-refreshes — netWorth/streak/topMover are insensitive to back-dated expenses anyway since they're per-month/lifetime aggregates, not strictly-most-recent. The cheaper signal beats the precise one here · Impact: zero false-positive invalidations during scroll/tab-switch (which is the whole point of the cache); one corner case (back-dated insert without refresh) tolerates ≤ 30 s stale dashboard values.
- 2026-05-18 · 2.14 fetchers held in a `useRef` updated each render rather than passed via deps · The per-feature provider value objects are rebuilt on every state change in their slice, so `netWorth`/`streakDays`/etc. get fresh function identities multiple times per session. Listing them as deps would re-create `load` and re-fire the version effect on every parent re-render — pathological. Ref-based latest-fetchers gives `load` a stable identity (empty dep array) while still calling the freshest fetcher · Impact: the version effect fires exactly when `expenses[0]?.id` changes — not on every parent render. Same pattern is reusable for future hooks that compose async repo calls.
- 2026-05-18 · 2.15 deleted UI.js outright instead of keeping a barrel re-export shim · The 7 unused exports (Card/Btn/Chip/SectionHeader/StatTile/PotRing/DonutChart) have zero importers across `app/src` — they were dead code, not "future API". Rule 9 (no churn for unused code) wins over Rule 14 (preserve future flexibility) when the artifact is genuinely unused today. Phase 2/3 screens that need a Btn or StatTile will rebuild them with current design tokens rather than inherit shapes from a pre-RefreshBus era · Impact: 173-LOC drop. If a Phase-2 task does want Card/Btn/etc., the previous shapes live in `git log -- app/src/components/UI.js` indefinitely.
- 2026-05-18 · 2.15 chose one primitive per file rather than a `primitives/index.js` re-export barrel · Each primitive is a single export with its own React.memo wrapper; barrel imports would defeat tree-shaking on web targets (none today but Drift web is on the long-term roadmap) and add an indirection step for IDE jump-to-definition. The 9 importer sites each take a single named import from a single file — clearer at the call site than `import { ProgressBar } from '@components/primitives'` · Impact: 4 primitive files for 4 used components. New primitives (when Phase 2 polish lands) land as new files in the same folder, no `index.js` to keep in sync.
- 2026-05-18 · 2.16 closed as no-op rather than reopened to sweep aliases · The XS spec was literally "update ItemRows to import from `core/domain/` not `ocr/`". QW-10 already moved the files and updated the import — that line item is satisfied. The alternative scope ("also sweep relative paths to `@core/` alias") was a reasonable extension but isn't what the task asked for, and the file is currently working; per Rule 9 the alias sweep belongs to a "next time ItemRows is touched" cleanup, not its own task · Impact: ItemRows imports remain at the longer relative form (`../core/domain/normalize`) like ~6 other untouched files. The aliasing convergence stays incremental.
- 2026-05-18 · 2.17 React Profiler verification deferred to a hands-on session rather than gating the task · Verification needs a connected device + DevTools profile session — not something I can run in this loop. Wrapping 21 screens in `React.memo` is the wrong place to require a measurement gate: each wrap is a pure perf-or-noop change with zero correctness risk (memo's only failure mode is reduced cache hit rate, never wrong output). The user's option 1 ("All 21 screens") explicitly accepted this trade-off · Impact: once any user runs the app with DevTools attached, Home/Trends/Subs/Scan should show fewer re-render cycles on tab switches and on `useApp()` mutations that don't affect their slice. If a screen ends up needing a custom equality fn (e.g. comparing route.params deeply), that's a per-screen follow-up.
- 2026-05-18 · 2.17 used `function X + export default React.memo(X)` rather than the inline `const X = React.memo(function X(){})` form QW-19 used for primitives · Inline form is denser but it forces the memo wrapper to be the only export — fine for primitives, but several screens have inner helpers (`FilterPill` in AllExpenses, `Section` in NetWorth, `ManageList`/`EditOne` in EditPot, `ItemEditor` in Scan, `Field`+`input` in EditSub, `Field`+`inputStyle`+`chipStyle` in EditTrip, `tripLengthDays` in Travel, `Row` in Profile) that share file scope with the default export. Two-line `function X(...); export default React.memo(X);` keeps the helper layout untouched and gives DevTools a named-function display (`X`, not `Anonymous`) · Impact: 21 mechanical rewrites, zero structural change to any of the 8 files with internal helpers. The diff for each is exactly two edits (param list rename + EOF append).
- 2026-05-18 · 3.1–3.10 batched as 10 separate migration objects (v2..v11), one per task ID, not collapsed into a single mega-v2 · User-selected. Keeps each version independently stamped in `schema_version`, mirrors the v1 baseline pattern, and lets a future failure pinpoint exactly which migration broke. Cost: ~10 trivial objects with `version`, `name`, `up`. Benefit: per-version recoverability and a clean audit trail · Impact: schema.js gains ~180 lines (SQL constants + migration objects) but stays readable; the runner change for v10 (see below) is the only structural addition.
- 2026-05-18 · 3.1 deleted_at columns landed without partial indexes (`WHERE deleted_at IS NULL`) · The execution_roadmap and tracker spec list no such index. Adding one preemptively would (a) bloat every write path with an extra index maintenance entry for a predicate that's currently never queried, (b) cement a query pattern (`WHERE deleted_at IS NULL` only) that 3.15's repo rewrite may end up using differently (e.g. range-predicates with month_key + a covering index). Defer to a profiler-driven decision in 3.15 · Impact: soft-delete column reads cost the same as any nullable column. If list queries grow slow once 3.15 adds the predicate, a partial index can be added in its own migration cheaply.
- 2026-05-18 · 3.2 month_key chose VIRTUAL over STORED · User-selected. Existing-row migration is O(1) (column metadata only, no row rewrite), index works the same either way (SQLite ≥ 3.31 supports indexes on both kinds). The cost is recompute-on-read for non-indexed paths, but every query that uses `month_key` will hit `idx_exp_month` or `idx_exp_month_cat`, so the compute happens at index-build time, not at query time · Impact: migration completes instantly on a 50k-row table; storage unchanged; STORED would have doubled the row-size penalty of the column data with no read benefit on indexed queries.
- 2026-05-18 · 3.3 / 3.4 merchants and products tables kept to the minimal v1 shape (id, name, canonical_name, created_at) · User-selected. Speculative columns (category_id, default_gst_rate, logo_emoji, notes) would have anchored a design that Phase-2 features may not actually need; better to extend each table in a dedicated future migration once a concrete consumer exists (merchant autocomplete in 5.9, GST persistence in 5.11, product price history in 6.5). The FK from `expenses.merchant_id` / `receipt_items.product_id` remains valid regardless of future column adds · Impact: smallest possible surface area to introduce these tables; future migrations will ADD COLUMN incrementally as features land — no rebuild needed.
- 2026-05-18 · 3.4 `products_fts` FTS5 table created without sync triggers · Sync triggers (INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE on products → products_fts) live with 3.13's overall FTS hookup work (which also covers `item_fts` over receipt_items). Creating the table now without triggers means it stays empty until 3.13 or until callers manually push rows via `INSERT INTO products_fts(rowid, name, canonical_name) VALUES (...)` — verified working in the dry-run · Impact: 3.4 lands the schema; 3.13 lands the wiring. No queries break because no consumer references products_fts yet.
- 2026-05-18 · 3.5 nullable FK columns declared with explicit `REFERENCES … ON DELETE SET NULL` rather than plain INTEGER · User-selected. `db/index.js` already enforces FKs via `PRAGMA foreign_keys = ON`, so the declarations actually take effect — deleting an account/trip/subscription will null the column on associated expenses instead of orphaning. Application-only integrity (plain INTEGER) loses that guarantee for the sake of marginally cheaper writes · Impact: write-time FK lookups add a tiny per-row cost (negligible for ≤100k rows), reads unchanged. If a future bulk-import wants to skip FK enforcement, it can `PRAGMA foreign_keys = OFF` within a transactionless migration the same way v10 does.
- 2026-05-18 · 3.5 `expenses.currency` default = NULL, not 'INR' · User-selected. NULL = "inherit profile/settings.currency at read time"; a literal default would have backfilled every historical expense with whatever the current setting is, baking that decision permanently. With NULL, a future settings change retroactively reinterprets the historical view (which is what most users want; the alternative requires a UI to retro-fix every row). `amount_home` and `fx_rate` follow the same NULL default — historical rows have only `amount` and that's read as-is · Impact: queries must `COALESCE(currency, (SELECT currency FROM settings))` to surface display currency; minor extra cost vs. column-only read. The complexity buys correctness on currency-switch.
- 2026-05-18 · 3.7 / 3.8 `account_transactions` and `goal_contributions` include nullable `expense_id` link (FK ON DELETE SET NULL) · User-selected. Lets the ledger tables optionally point back to the originating expense for drill-through (e.g. "this account debit came from the Zomato expense"). ON DELETE SET NULL means deleting the expense doesn't lose the ledger row — important because the ledger is the source of truth for account balance reconciliation. The link is optional, so externally-recorded transactions (e.g. interest, bank fees) stay supported · Impact: enables a Phase-2 "view source expense" affordance without forcing it; ledger tables remain valid even when the originating expense is purged.
- 2026-05-18 · 3.9 runner extension: added `transactionless: true` flag instead of carving v10 out as a special case · User-selected. SQLite's `PRAGMA foreign_keys` cannot toggle inside a multi-statement transaction (the runtime ignores the pragma if a transaction is open), so the v10 table rebuild needs autocommit context. The minimally invasive fix is a 15-line `if (mig.transactionless) { ... } else { withTransactionAsync(...) }` branch in `runMigrations()`: the migration owns its own transaction discipline, the runner stamps schema_version after the migration's COMMIT. The migration must be idempotent on retry (stamp insert can still fail after the rebuild succeeded) — handled by `DROP TABLE IF EXISTS subscriptions_new` at the top of v10 and an `INSERT … SELECT` that works whether subscriptions has the old or new shape · Impact: future rebuilds (e.g. when a Phase-3 task adds a new CHECK constraint to expenses) reuse the same flag with no further runner change. Stamping race documented in `db/index.js` comment.
- 2026-05-18 · 3.9 verdict value coercion: rows with `verdict NOT IN ('keep','cancel','review')` silently rewritten to 'keep' during INSERT … SELECT, not rejected · User-selected (implicit by approval of the plan). Failing the migration on bad data would brick the app for any user whose subscriptions table has a verdict outside the new enum — and since v1 had no CHECK, any string is technically valid pre-v10. Coercing to 'keep' (the safe default) preserves the row and the user can re-categorise from the Subs UI. Dry-run confirmed the coercion path with a seeded `verdict='pause'` row · Impact: data preservation > strict validation. Worst case: a user who deliberately typed 'pause' for "consider pausing" loses that bookkeeping. Acceptable since the Subs UI has no such option and the value would have been from a debug session.
- 2026-05-18 · 3.9 subscriptions_new uses a hand-rolled column list rather than `CREATE TABLE … AS SELECT * FROM subscriptions` · The AS-SELECT shortcut drops constraints, defaults, and FKs (it just copies type-affinity + data). Hand-rolling the column list lets us redeclare every constraint (NOT NULL defaults, the new CHECK, the new linked_category_id FK) and matches the SQLite docs' official table-alteration recipe verbatim · Impact: schema integrity preserved across the rebuild; the post-migration `PRAGMA table_info('subscriptions')` shows all expected columns with their constraints, verified in the dry-run.
- 2026-05-18 · 3.10 `budget_period` declared NOT NULL with DEFAULT 'month' · The CHECK applies to all rows including historical (existing rows get the default value at ADD-COLUMN time per SQLite semantics). NOT NULL ensures future inserts can't bypass the CHECK with NULL. The three allowed values match the Phase-4 rollover budgets work (7.10) which will need monthly/weekly/yearly cadence selection. Defaulting to 'month' preserves all existing budget-tracking behavior · Impact: zero behavioural change today (the column is unused); future budget rollover work has a typed cadence to read from without retro-migrating every category row.
- 2026-05-18 · 3.1–3.10 V1_REQUIRED_TABLES intentionally left frozen at v1's 10 tables · Adding the new tables (merchants, products, account_transactions, goal_contributions) here would break legacy-stamp detection on installs created before v2 existed — those installs would no longer match the "all v1 tables present" check and would fall through to v1.up (safe, but unnecessary churn). The TABLES export (used by `resetAll()`) DID get the new tables, in children-first order so resetAll FK cascades stay valid · Impact: legacy-stamp logic stays correct across all future migrations; only TABLES grows with each new table. Comment added in schema.js to lock this convention.
- 2026-05-18 · 3.1–3.10 deferred application to next user-initiated app launch (validation done via node:sqlite, not by running the app) · User-selected. The migration ladder was dry-run against an in-memory SQLite seeded with a verdict='pause' row + an expense linked via subscription_id, exercising the v10 FK-survival and verdict-coercion paths. Running against the real `drift.db` is the user's call — backing it up before next launch is recommended (and noted in the implementation summary) · Impact: zero risk of mid-session corruption from this session; user retains full control over when migrations apply.
- 2026-05-18 · 3.11 chose `category_id INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0` sentinel over `category_id NULLABLE` in `monthly_summary` · SQLite allows multiple NULLs in a PRIMARY KEY (historical bug, never fixed), so `PRIMARY KEY (month_key, category_id)` with NULL category_ids would not enforce one row per (month, uncategorised) bucket — every NULL-category expense would create a new row. AUTOINCREMENT starts at 1 so id=0 is a safe sentinel for uncategorised. Triggers `COALESCE(NEW.category_id, 0)` everywhere they map expense→bucket · Impact: aggregations group correctly; consumers (3.19's summaryByCategory) join via the same COALESCE on c.id which never returns 0 (real categories start at 1), so uncategorised rolls into the LEFT JOIN's "no match" branch and contributes only via `(0,…)` rollup rows. If we later add a synthetic "Uncategorised" category in the categories table, it'd have id ≥ 1 and the rollup behaviour stays consistent.
- 2026-05-18 · 3.11 split v12/v13/v14/v15 across 4 migrations instead of one mega-v12 · Mirrors the v2..v11 batching convention from 3.1–3.10. Each migration is independently stamped in `schema_version`; a future failure pinpoints exactly which artefact broke (rollups vs expense FTS vs item FTS vs backfill). v15 is the only one whose semantics differ on first-run (no-op on fresh) vs upgrade (one-shot populate from existing data) · Impact: schema.js grows by ~330 LOC; the 4 stamps make the audit trail honest.
- 2026-05-18 · 3.11 item_summary AU trigger handles the OLD.normalized_name == NEW.normalized_name same-bucket case via decrement-then-UPSERT-increment, which transiently passes points_count=0 and can DELETE the row before the UPSERT re-creates it · Traced via dry-run: when N=1 (only row for that name), step 1 decrements to 0, step 2 deletes the row, step 3's recompute UPDATE is a no-op, step 4 INSERT-ON-CONFLICT becomes a plain INSERT seeded from NEW.* with `points_count = 1`. End state is correct (1 row, NEW values). When N>1, step 2 doesn't fire, step 3 recomputes last_* from remaining live rows (which now include the post-UPDATE NEW row), step 4 ON-CONFLICT increments. End state is also correct · Impact: trigger is correct in all transitions but transiently inconsistent within the trigger body itself. Safe because triggers are atomic with their host statement — no other reader can observe the intermediate state.
- 2026-05-18 · 3.11 / 3.19 monthly_summary rollup is soft-delete-aware → no `deleted_at IS NULL` predicate on the rollup side in 3.19's rewrites · Triggers maintain the invariant: every row contributing to `monthly_summary.total` has `expenses.deleted_at IS NULL`. Filtering on the rollup would be a double-negative no-op. Confirmed by the dry-run: `SUM(monthly_summary.total)` exactly equals `SUM(expenses.amount WHERE deleted_at IS NULL)` after each mutation · Impact: simpler queries in `summaryByCategory` / `monthlyTrend` / `trackedItems` / `suggest`. If a future task introduces a "view deleted" surface that wants to see soft-deleted aggregates, it would need a parallel rollup or a different query path — flagged as a constraint for the eventual Recycle Bin feature.
- 2026-05-18 · 3.11 FTS5 triggers evict soft-deleted rows (user-selected) instead of leaving them indexed · Consistent with the rollup choice. The trade-off: search results only surface live data, which means a future Recycle Bin can't reuse the FTS index for soft-deleted entries — it'd need either a separate FTS view or to drop the predicate temporarily. Acceptable for the current scope where soft-delete is not yet user-visible · Impact: AD triggers do `INSERT INTO expense_fts(expense_fts, rowid, ...) VALUES ('delete', ...)`; AU does delete-old + insert-new gated on each side's deleted_at. When a row is revived (deleted_at NULL → NULL), AU re-inserts the FTS row.
- 2026-05-18 · 3.17 deviated from "rewritten with FIRST_VALUE() OVER" to "read directly from item_summary" · The spec was written before item_summary existed (drafted in execution_roadmap.md which lists 3.11 separately from 3.17). With item_summary now storing `display_name`, `kind`, `last_unit`, `last_unit_price`, `last_canonical_unit`, `last_seen` per `normalized_name`, FIRST_VALUE OVER `receipt_items` would re-derive what's already materialised. The end goal — "no correlated subqueries" — is satisfied more directly · Impact: suggest() is now a single indexed PK-prefix lookup (`WHERE normalized_name LIKE 'x%'` uses item_summary's PK index when the pattern is a literal prefix). Strictly faster than the old query and the FIRST_VALUE alternative. The FIRST_VALUE form is preserved in the v15 backfill SQL, so the pattern is documented in the codebase for future reuse.
- 2026-05-18 · 3.16 kept `total_qty_30d` as a per-row live query against `receipt_items` instead of materialising into item_summary · The 30-day window is sliding (today-relative); a stored value would be stale within hours and require either a daily maintenance job to refresh or every read to invalidate. Live per-row aggregate is acceptable because trackedItems is a screen-load operation (not a list-render hot path) and the 30-day filter uses `idx_items_name_date` (already exists) · Impact: same number of queries as before (one main + N per-row spark + N per-row 30d). Future task can pre-materialise into a `rolling_30d` column if profiling shows it matters; today's data scale (<10k items) makes it not worth the complexity.
- 2026-05-18 · 1.C.20 helper-only path (no ESLint) per user-selected option · Codebase has no `.eslintrc*` / `eslint.config.*` and no `eslint` dep today. Adding ESLint just to enforce one rule would be 30-50 lines of config + custom-rule scaffolding + new devDep — more weight than the safety it buys when the helper is a one-line copy-paste at every list query. The 4 exported predicate constants from `db/predicates.js` document the intent in-place and pattern-match for code review · Impact: a missing predicate in a future list query is a code-review catch, not a CI catch. If predicate drift becomes a real problem (>1 incident), the file is structured for an ESLint rule to be added later without further refactor — the constants are the regex anchor.
- 2026-05-18 · 1.C.20 by-id `get(id)` paths intentionally left unfiltered · The literal spec said "every list query"; `get(id)` is a single-row primary-key lookup, not a list. Filtering it would either silently return null for soft-deleted IDs (subtle UX regression — "where did my expense go?") or require every caller to handle both shapes. Today no mutation path writes `deleted_at`, so the question is moot, but the conservative choice preserves the current behaviour. The flip can land alongside whichever task introduces user-facing soft-delete (Recycle Bin / Undo) · Impact: `get(id)` and `update(id, patch)` will still resolve soft-deleted rows until that future task arrives. Acceptable because no current code soft-deletes anything.
- 2026-05-19 · 4.5 plausibility window set to `year ∈ [2000, currentYear+1]` rather than [1990, currentYear] · A `+1`-year head-room covers the common end-of-year reality: a Dec 31 receipt clocked by the camera as Jan 1 next year (timezone drift, or photo metadata after midnight). Pre-2000 rejected because Drift's user base is on smartphones; there are no 1990s receipts being scanned · Impact: years 2027 (when this lands; today's currentYear+1) and beyond will continue to be accepted as the system clock rolls forward — the window auto-advances. A receipt with a typo'd year of 2099 still falls through to the orchestrator's fallback (today's date).
- 2026-05-19 · 4.5 walk-every-match strategy in `findDate` rather than first-match-only · An OCR string like "Bill: 32/13/2026 ... GSTIN ... 15 May 2026" should land on `15 May 2026`. The previous first-match-only code would have returned `null` from the bad `32/13/2026` and then never tried `MONTH_DATE_RE`. With the `g`-flag walk, both regexes are exhausted before falling back. Cost: bounded by the number of date-shaped fragments per receipt (typically 1–3) · Impact: poison-pill date strings no longer derail a perfectly extractable receipt date.
- 2026-05-19 · 4.6 trailing-price threshold = 0.7 (last 30% of the line) rather than the more literal "amount must be the rightmost token" · An exact-rightmost rule would mis-classify "Total ₹1,234.50" (1 amount, trivially OK) but also mis-classify multi-amount lines like "CGST @9% ₹45.00" if interpreted strictly. The "last 30%" heuristic handles both correctly: with one amount, the function short-circuits to trailing=true; with multiple amounts, only the last one's *position* matters. 30% specifically because most receipt rows put labels on the left and amounts hard-aligned to the right · Impact: bill rows where the keyword sits in the middle of a longer free-text label ("Total Mix Snack 4 x 50 200") correctly classify as `item`. Edge case: a very short label like "Tax 45" (4 chars before amount) — 4/8 = 0.5, less than 0.7, would classify as `item`. Acceptable because the corresponding TAX bottom-band detection in `parseReceipt`'s fallback path still recovers the total via reconciliation.
- 2026-05-19 · 4.6 `meta` test moved BEFORE the trailing-price gate · "Order ID: ABC123" has no amounts and a mid-string price-position would be undefined; testing META_RE first short-circuits cleanly and keeps `meta` behaviour identical to pre-rename. Same for `bill_header` — those never carry amounts · Impact: zero classification change for meta / bill_header rows. The new rule only affects the 5 amount-bearing categories.
- 2026-05-19 · 4.7 5% RELATIVE tolerance rather than absolute · The previous code used `Math.abs(d - round(d)) < 0.05` which is absolute — that breaks down for fractional snaps (a value of 0.30 is 0.05 absolute from 0.25, accepting it would wrongly snap). Relative tolerance scales with the target: 5% of 0.25 = 0.0125 (so 0.30 correctly rejected), 5% of 50 = 2.5 (so 50.5 correctly snaps to 50). The implementation: `Math.abs(derived - target) / target <= 0.05` · Impact: integer behaviour is essentially the same as before (5% of 1 = 0.05, identical bound); fractional behaviour is strict, no over-snap regression.
- 2026-05-19 · 4.7 fraction set = `{0.25, 0.5, 0.75}` only (no thirds, no eighths) · User-selected. Indian retail receipts deal almost exclusively in halves and quarters (half-kg sugar, quarter-litre milk). Thirds (1/3 = 0.333) would create ambiguity with OCR'd 0.30 (real qty); eighths are vanishingly rare. Pharmacy split-strip cases that genuinely need 1/3 will continue to surface as the raw derived value (no snap, qtyHint=null → tabular extractor falls through to the trailing-1-2-digit-integer pickup) · Impact: cleaner predictability. If thirds become a real signal later, the FRACTIONAL_SNAPS array is a one-line extension.
- 2026-05-19 · 4.2 seed data smaller (~5KB each) than the tracker's `~80KB / ~50KB` estimates · The bytes estimate in execution_roadmap.md was speculative; in practice 150 Hinglish staples + 95 Devanagari entries is enough to cover the daily-staples corpus that drives most repeat-purchase analytics. Brand-tail (Amul Gold, Mother Dairy products), pharmacy (drug names), and regional vegetables (Tamil/Telugu/Bengali equivalents) are the natural growth axes — they should land in their own content PRs with translator review, not crammed into this scaffolding task · Impact: schema/loader contract is finalised; the JSON files double in size painlessly as content PRs grow. No code change needed when content grows.
- 2026-05-19 · 4.2 whole-string lookup ONLY (no substring/word-substitution) · Substring substitution would silently rewrite parts of compound names: "Amul Doodh 1L" → "Amul milk" — which loses the brand context that price-history-per-merchant relies on. Whole-string lookup is conservative: only exact matches (after NFC + lowercase) are canonicalised, everything else passes through unchanged. Brand+staple combos like "Mother Dairy Doodh" stay distinct from the bare "Doodh" → "milk" canonical · Impact: predictable, no surprise renames. The user can add a `"amul doodh": "milk"` entry to the JSON if they specifically want that collapse for a given brand+staple pair — explicit > implicit.
- 2026-05-19 · 4.2 `canonicalizeName` chains Hindi → synonyms (two-stage) rather than a single flat map · Some entries chain through Devanagari → Hinglish (already in hindi_product_map) → English staple (caught by product_synonyms). For example: `दूध` → `milk` is a one-shot; but if a future entry maps `दूध फुल क्रीम` → `doodh full cream` (Devanagari → Hinglish) and `doodh full cream` is already in synonyms (→ `milk`), the two-stage pipeline catches both. Each map can be authored against the alphabet its translator knows best · Impact: content PRs can add Devanagari entries that point to existing Hinglish keys without needing English coverage. Saves duplicate maintenance.
- 2026-05-19 · 4.8 ScanService passes a separate `taxInvoice` state object rather than spreading every field as a top-level state · Five new useState calls in Scan.js would have crowded the existing 11 hooks past the threshold where the component is comprehensible. A single `taxInvoice = { gstin, invoice_number, cgst, sgst, igst }` slice spreads cleanly into `addExpenseWithItems({ expense: {...base, ...(taxInvoice||{}) }})` and is null-able in one place for resetScreen. Pattern matches how `parsed` carries those fields end-to-end · Impact: zero render perf difference (1 useState vs 5 + spread); cleaner reset path; trivial to add a "Tax details" expansion section in 5.11.
- 2026-05-19 · 4.8 CGST/SGST/IGST split into 3 columns rather than a `tax_breakdown` JSON column · User-selected. The analytics roadmap (Phase 3 — inflation index, lifestyle drift) does not yet query by tax type, but the column-typed shape sets up clean SQL aggregation when it does. A JSON column would force substring queries (`WHERE json_extract(tax_breakdown, '$.cgst') > 0`) — slower, no index, harder to read in a debug console. Three nullable REAL columns cost ~24 bytes per row on the wire, zero query-plan complexity · Impact: schema is slightly wider; query patterns stay first-class SQL. The bundled `tax` column from v1 stays as a denormalised sum for back-compat with the existing confidence/reconcile logic.
- 2026-05-19 · 4.8 manual Add/EditExpense paths intentionally NOT extended with tax fields · The 5 new columns are populated ONLY by the Scan flow today (auto-extracted from the receipt OCR). Manual entry doesn't surface a "GSTIN" / "invoice number" field, and shouldn't — those are receipt artefacts, not user-typed data. EditExpense's `update()` SQL doesn't touch the new columns either (SQL UPDATE leaves unmentioned columns alone), so a Scan-created expense that the user later edits via EditExpense KEEPS its `gstin` / `cgst` etc. Detail screen will surface them in 5.11 · Impact: minimal surface change; the 5 columns behave as Scan-flow-only metadata. If a future task wants a manual "edit tax breakdown" UI, it adds the fields to EditExpense's form + extends update() in one go.
- 2026-05-19 · 4.8 receipt_items.hsn populated by per-row HSN_RE scan during item extraction rather than via a global pass · HSN codes in tabular receipts (DMart, Reliance) appear on the same physical row as the item — `HSN: 10063020` after "Basmati Rice 5kg 500". Scanning each row's text in `extractItemsCard / Tabular / Permissive` correctly attributes the code to the line item. A global single-HSN pass would have either picked the first HSN on the receipt for every item (wrong) or required complex y-coordinate matching (over-engineering for an opt-in field) · Impact: each item gets its own HSN when the receipt provides one inline; items without a per-row HSN get NULL. Pharmacy receipts (one HSN per drug) work; restaurant bills (zero HSN) work; mixed-rate departmental bills (HSN per row) work correctly.
- 2026-05-19 · 4.8 INVOICE_NO_RE introduced separately from ORDER_ID_RE · They're different documents on Indian receipts: ORDER_ID is the platform's internal ID (Amazon/Flipkart), INVOICE_NUMBER is the GST-mandated tax document number. Reusing ORDER_ID_RE would mis-populate `invoice_number` with random order IDs for non-invoice receipts (e.g. food delivery never has an invoice). Two regexes keep the two columns honest; lines where only ORDER_ID is present leave `invoice_number` NULL · Impact: `parsed.invoiceNumber` is null on receipts without an explicit "Invoice No:" line — correct for food-delivery / quick-commerce / handwritten cases. GST-invoice formats (departmental, restaurant, pharmacy) populate it.
- 2026-05-19 · 4.8 CGST_AMOUNT_RE rate-prefix tightened to REQUIRE trailing `%` after the first test run · The initial draft used `(?:\\s*@?\\s*\\d+(?:\\.\\d+)?%?)?` — but `%?` makes the percent optional, so the rate-prefix would greedily eat "45.00" thinking it's the rate. Tightened to `(?:\\s*@?\\s*\\d+(?:\\.\\d+)?\\s*%)?` (`%` mandatory inside the optional group). The whole group is still optional — receipts with no rate ("CGST 45.00") match cleanly; with-rate cases ("CGST @9% 45.00", "CGST 18% 180.00") match because the `%` is present · Impact: 1 test went from failure to pass; no regression on the other 8 cases. Lesson logged for future receipt-extraction regex work: optional groups must REQUIRE a structural anchor that the following pattern can't itself supply.
- 2026-05-19 · 4.9 Latin-only by default (Devanagari recogniser not registered) · User-selected option. ML Kit v2 exposes per-recogniser script selection via `TextRecognition.recognize(uri, script)`. Running both Latin + Devanagari per scan is roughly 2× the OCR cost — battery, latency, memory. Hindi text on Indian receipts is already handled at the canonicaliser level (4.2: `दूध / Doodh / Milk` all whole-string-map to `milk`) so the Latin recogniser usually has enough signal. The script parameter is plumbed through `recognize(uri, {script})` so a future "Scan as Hindi" toggle or low-confidence-auto-fallback can wire it in without re-writing the bridge · Impact: zero perf regression for the 99% English-receipt case. Hindi-only receipts will OCR worse than they could; that's an explicit deferred tradeoff, documented for revisit when 4.19's golden dataset includes Hindi samples.
- 2026-05-19 · 4.10 reads `el.confidence ?? 1.0` even though v2 native bridge doesn't currently surface it · ML Kit Android's `Text.Element.getConfidence()` exists and is documented, but the upstream `@react-native-ml-kit/text-recognition@2.0.0` bridge (verified by inspecting `android/src/main/java/com/rnmlkit/textrecognition/TextRecognitionModule.java#lineToMap` and `index.ts`) does NOT pipe it through to JS — only text, frame, cornerPoints, and recognizedLanguages cross the bridge. We read it defensively (`el.confidence ?? 1.0`) so the consumer side is correct the moment (a) the upstream lib starts exposing it or (b) we patch the native bridge ourselves. Defaulting absent to 1.0 (not 0) matches the "no signal" semantics — downstream consumers (auto-Devanagari fallback, scoreConfidence weighting, golden-dataset gating) treat 1.0 as "we have no reason to distrust this line" rather than as "this line is broken" · Impact: code shape ships, the data flow is correct, but the field is effectively dead until the bridge surfaces real values. Tagged in the Completion log so the next person diagnosing why confidence is always 1.0 doesn't waste time.
- 2026-05-19 · 4.11 expo-image-manipulator pinned to ~14.0.8 (SDK 54 match) rather than ^55.0.16 (npm latest) · Expo SDK modules use independent versioning anchored to the SDK they ship with. `npm install` resolved `^55.0.16` (latest), which pairs with SDK 55 and may have native ABI mismatches against the project's `expo@~54.0.33`. `npx expo install --check` flagged the mismatch and prescribed `~14.0.8`. Lesson: prefer `npx expo install <pkg>` for any module under the `expo-*` namespace · Impact: no runtime regression from the latest version — v14 still exposes `manipulateAsync` (just marked deprecated in favour of a new contextual API; switching is a Phase 5+ task, not a 4.11 concern).
- 2026-05-19 · 4.11 single resize action (width=1600), no rotate / EXIF strip / crop · The receipt image pipeline foundation (5.12) and the full preprocess (4.20) are the proper homes for orientation handling, EXIF stripping, and grayscale/CLAHE/Sauvola/deskew. 4.11's contract is "light preprocess" — resize + PNG re-save, nothing else. Adding rotate or crop here would creep into 5.12/4.20 work and would need to be re-done when those land. The PNG re-save alone is meaningful: it strips JPEG quantisation artefacts that bleed into glyph edges, which helps ML Kit's segmentation · Impact: 4.20's native preprocess will REPLACE this helper entirely, not extend it. The Scan call site will swap one import for another; behaviour will be a strict improvement. Until 4.20, lightPreprocess is a Pareto-improvement over passing the raw JPEG.
- 2026-05-19 · 4.13 auto-fallback signalled by `null` return, not `[]` · `extractItemsColumnar` returning `[]` means "this strategy ran cleanly and the receipt genuinely has no items" (e.g. a coffee shop with one charge). `null` means "I couldn't make sense of the column structure; please try a different strategy". The dispatcher distinguishes the two — only `null` triggers fallback to `extractItemsTabular`. If we used `[]` as the fallback signal, a legitimately empty receipt would silently double-process through tabular, which could fabricate phantom items from header/footer rows · Impact: the auto-fallback path is conservative — columnar gets first try, falls through only on structural failure, no risk of producing more items than the input warrants.
- 2026-05-19 · 4.13 + 4.12 mergeIntoRows token-flattening done as part of 4.12 (not 4.13) · Strictly the per-token list is a 4.13 dependency, but the natural place to flatten elements is the same merge step that already groups lines by y-overlap. Doing it in 4.12 (where the columns module lives) keeps the columns module pure (no awareness of OCR plumbing) and makes the `tokens` field available to any future column-aware code without each consumer re-walking `elements[]` · Impact: zero added cost on receipts that don't use columnar extraction — tokens is computed once per row, then read only when columnar runs.
- 2026-05-19 · 4.14 dedup hashes computed from in-memory parsed shape just before save, NOT from OCR-time `parsed` · OCR may extract a wrong merchant or off-by-one date; users correct these on the review screen before tapping Save. Hashing at OCR time would mean two different OCR runs of the same bill produce different hashes (because each OCR run gets random merchant guesses on noisy receipts). Hashing at save time captures the user-corrected truth, so re-scanning the same bill after editing produces the same hash and gets flagged · Impact: deduplication is robust to OCR noise as long as the user corrects merchant/date consistently. Side effect: if the user edits merchant differently on two scans of the same bill, the dedup misses — but that's the same human-error case any dedup scheme would miss, and the soft hash (merchant+rupee-rounded total) still catches the common case.
- 2026-05-19 · 4.14 djb2 32-bit hash, 8-hex string, no crypto · The use case is intra-user duplicate detection, not authentication. djb2 is fast, has well-understood distribution properties, and the 32-bit space (~4B values) is plenty for a single user's lifetime of receipt scans. expo-crypto would add a native module dependency for no security benefit. The 8-hex form is human-debuggable in `sqlite3` query inspectors. The collision risk is ~4B^-1 per pair → vanishing for any single user · Impact: zero new native dependencies. If collisions ever become a real concern (multi-user cloud-sync scenarios in Phase 5+), upgrading to SHA-1 is a one-function-body change.
- 2026-05-19 · 4.14 useExpenses() exposes findDuplicate directly, not via useApp() · CLAUDE.md explicitly says "new code should import the per-feature hook directly". The legacy `useApp()` aggregator is back-compat-only. Scan.js was already using `useApp()` for its existing data so we kept that import to avoid Rule-9 churn, but for the NEW findDuplicate call we used `useExpenses()` directly. Result: only the per-feature subscriber re-renders when expenses change, not the entire aggregator. Pattern matches the 2.10 refactor's direction of travel · Impact: each NEW screen-level data dependency lands as a per-feature import going forward; useApp() shrinks over time without a forced sweep.
- 2026-05-19 · 4.15 merchant resolution happens inside expenses.createWithItems' transaction, not before it · expo-sqlite uses a single cached connection (see `db/index.js`'s `_db = null` memoisation). Calling `merchants.resolve(name)` while inside `db.withTransactionAsync(...)` participates in the same transaction — so the merchants INSERT and the expenses INSERT either both succeed or both roll back. Calling it BEFORE the transaction would leave orphaned merchants rows on a downstream failure (e.g. an items INSERT throws after the expenses INSERT). The cost is one extra round-trip on each scan, but those are sub-ms on local SQLite · Impact: write atomicity is preserved end-to-end. A future bulk-import path that doesn't use createWithItems will need to opt into the same transactional discipline.
- 2026-05-19 · 4.15 expenses.update() NOT extended to re-resolve merchant_id when the user edits the merchant string · Mirroring the 4.8 decision (manual paths not extended). The merchant_id FK was added in v4 specifically for the merchant-intelligence layer (5.9 — MerchantDetailScreen + top-merchants query). Today nothing consumes it except 5.9 will. Adding re-resolution to update() now would mean every manual edit to a typo in a merchant name triggers a SELECT + JW walk + maybe-INSERT. That's defensible work, but it belongs in 5.9 where the consumer ships alongside · Impact: edits to a Scan-created expense's merchant string leave the original merchant_id pointing at the OCR-time merchant row. When 5.9 lands, we'll add a small migration that backfills merchant_id from `merchant` for all rows AND extend update() to re-resolve.
- 2026-05-19 · 4.16 fuel item emits with `kind: 'fuel'` and uses unit `l` · receipt_items.kind is unconstrained TEXT in v1 schema (no CHECK), so adding 'fuel' alongside 'produce' / 'grocery' / 'other' is a one-character extension. Unit `l` flows through the existing `toCanonical()` pipeline which already understands litres; canonical_unit = 'l' so per-litre price history is well-formed alongside the existing milk/oil items. Future fuel/vehicle tracking (7.6) can `SELECT … WHERE kind = 'fuel'` to materialise the dedicated vehicles + fuel_fillups tables · Impact: zero schema change. The fuel data is fully addressable by analytics today (price-per-litre rollup), and future fuel-specific UI consumes it through a stable kind filter.
- 2026-05-19 · 4.16 fuel format `expectItems` flipped from `false` to `true` · The original FORMAT_CONFIGS.fuel had `expectItems: false` because the previous extractor (`extractItemsTotalsOnly`) emitted nothing. Now that we emit a single item per receipt the flag should mean what it says: yes, this format produces items, and downstream confidence/reconcile logic should treat a missing item as a quality signal · Impact: confidence scoring now reflects fuel-item extraction success the same way it reflects grocery extraction success. A fuel receipt where extractFuelItem can't find liters × rate produces 0 items → confidence drops → "needs review" surfaces. That's the right UX.
- 2026-05-19 · 4.17 v17 adds cgst_rate + sgst_rate + igst_rate (3 columns), not just the 2 the tracker line names · User-selected option in the kickoff AskUserQuestion. Symmetric with v16's bill-level cgst/sgst/igst amounts; future-flexibility win when IGST-per-item gets interesting (interstate B2B receipts). Cost is one extra REAL column with NULL default — negligible storage on a single-user database · Impact: when the Detail screen UI lands (5.11) and the merchant-intelligence layer comes online (5.9), interstate per-item tax is already representable without a v18. No re-migration of historical rows needed because all three columns are nullable.
- 2026-05-19 · 4.17 bare-GST disambiguation: split symmetrically for intrastate, attribute to IGST for interstate, leave NULL when ambiguous · A bare "GST 5%" line on an item row is ambiguous until you know the bill's destination. Indian GST convention is that intrastate bills carry CGST+SGST (each half the combined rate); interstate bills carry IGST (full rate). We can read the bill-level taxBreakdown to decide. When neither CGST/SGST/IGST appears at the bill level (e.g. a non-GST-registered shop) we leave per-item rates NULL rather than guess — analytics is better off knowing "we don't know" than carrying fabricated data · Impact: per-item GST rates are well-defined or honestly absent. Future analytics over `receipt_items.cgst_rate` etc. won't be polluted by guesses. The disambiguation logic is centralised in the orchestrator (parseReceipt.js) so a future rule change (e.g. a destination override) is one diff.
- 2026-05-19 · 4.17 columnar Tax%-column detection NOT implemented in this task · Some Indian retail receipts (DMart, Reliance Smart) print a dedicated Tax% column. The current regex-based extractor catches "GST N%" mentions but not bare percentage values in a labelled column. Implementing column-header detection would require teaching the columnar strategy to read column headers and bucket values accordingly — meaningful work, separable concern · Impact: receipts with a dedicated Tax% column will currently produce per-item rates only when the row text also includes "GST" / "CGST" / etc. somewhere. This is a known coverage gap and is the kind of regression the golden dataset (4.19) is designed to surface — when real DMart fixtures land via the user-export pipeline and the harness shows the gap, that becomes a separate task.
- 2026-05-19 · 4.18 soft band stays at 2 × primary; subtotal band uses primary (all three bands scale together) · User-selected option in the kickoff AskUserQuestion. Preserves the three-tier semantics from the prior literal bands (primary = full credit, soft = half credit, subtotal = full credit on the items-incomplete fallback) at every bill size. Alternatives considered: (a) replace only primary, leave 0.15/0.07 hardcoded — odd cliff at long bills where the soft band becomes huge relative to primary; (b) drop the soft band entirely — confidence drops harder than necessary at 6–8% off · Impact: the reconciliation component scores smoothly across n. Confidence score behaviour is monotonic in `itemCount × |diff|` instead of having a fixed offset.
- 2026-05-19 · 4.19 marked [!] PARTIAL, not [x] · User-selected option in the kickoff AskUserQuestion. The task ID's scope is 50 anonymised receipt scans + expected outputs. We landed the runner, the auto-capture pipeline, the Profile export UX, the SOURCES.md shortlist, and 5 hand-built fixtures — but not the 50-fixture corpus, which is fundamentally a data-collection task. Marking [x] now would set a precedent that "infrastructure done = task done", which would erode the task tracker's signal. The [!] is honest: the work is partially done, the path to completion is concrete (user exports candidates → drops bundle into chat → ingest into fixtures/), and the gating condition is named · Impact: the tracker's percentage stays truthful; future-Claude reading this won't assume the golden dataset is available for regression-testing real Indian receipt edge cases. When the fixture count crosses 50 the line flips to [x] in the same commit that ingests the last batch.
- 2026-05-19 · 4.19 auto-capture default ON, no DB migration for the toggle · User-explicit zero-fiddle constraint. The Settings table at v1 has only currency/dark_mode/carbon_tracking columns; adding a `golden_capture` column would mean a v18 migration AND v17 → v18 versioning would need to be threaded through the schema_version stamp. Instead the toggle persists as a tiny JSON in `documentDirectory/drift/golden-candidates/.config.json` — the file lives in the same directory as the candidates, so a "Clear all captured" sweep can drop the whole dir without leaving an orphaned setting. Default ON (file absent → enabled=true) honors the user's stated preference · Impact: zero schema-evolution cost. Toggling is a single FS write. A future Settings-Sync feature (Phase 5) that wants to surface this toggle in the regular settings UI can add the column then; the FS file becomes the migration source.
- 2026-05-19 · 4.19 capture JSONs store structured OCR + parsed snapshots only, NOT images · The receipt image already lives at expense.receipt_uri (per 5.12); duplicating it in capture/ would be wasteful and would balloon export bundle sizes from ~10 KB to ~1 MB per scan. More importantly: the parser doesn't need images to regression-test — it consumes the ML Kit structured JSON (`blocks/lines/elements`), which is exactly what `scanAndProcess` already produces and what we store in the capture file. When the user exports, the bundle stays small and email/Drive-friendly. Privacy bonus: text-only captures contain merchant names + item names + dates but not handwriting, faces, hands, background — meaningfully less revealing if the bundle ever leaks · Impact: ~100× smaller bundles, ~zero re-OCR work to ingest, narrower privacy surface. The trade-off is we can't go back and re-OCR a problem case with a different preprocessor — but the moment the user has the original image saved elsewhere, that limitation is local. If 5.12 lands and we DO want to ingest images too, adding `imagePath` to the capture JSON is one field.
- 2026-05-19 · 4.19 capture writes are fire-and-forget on the save path · The save UX must never block on capture I/O. `writeCandidate(...)` is called with `.catch(() => {})` so any FS write failure, JSON serialisation throw, or quota issue is swallowed locally. The user's expense save remains the source of truth; the capture file is a best-effort log. Trade-off: a silently failed capture isn't reported to the user, but the user-visible promise is "save succeeded" — surfacing capture failures would create the false impression that the expense itself might be at risk · Impact: capture pipeline failures are invisible (correct for low-stakes background work). If the pipeline ever produces zero captures despite many scans, the user will notice via "Nothing captured yet" on the Export button — that's the catch-all signal.
- 2026-05-19 · 4.19 expo-sharing dep NOT added by Claude in this batch · Per Rule 1, dep additions require explicit confirmation. The user said "go" with the understanding that the dep question was conditional. The implementation uses `require('expo-sharing')` at click time so the Profile module imports cleanly without the dep; when the user taps Export and the dep is missing, an Alert shows the bundle path with the install hint. This keeps the codebase shippable today and lets the user run `cd app && npx expo install expo-sharing` whenever they want the share-sheet UX · Impact: the feature is shippable in its lazy-require form; when the user installs expo-sharing the same code lights up with no further changes. If they prefer not to install it, the bundle still exists on disk and they can pull it via a file manager.
- 2026-05-19 · 4.24 item dedup by normalized_name + price (±0.01) is the user-selected option; stricter qty+unit OR fuzzy-name+price alternatives explicitly rejected · Spec scope (Step 2 choice) is conservative dedup: two photos of the SAME printed line are highly likely to OCR with identical normalized_name + identical price (the OCR'd amount is the most stable signal across re-captures). Including qty + unit in the key would un-dedup when one page's OCR jittered the qty field — net effect: more duplicates in the merged output. Jaro-Winkler on names would dedup more aggressively but with higher false-collapse risk (two distinct items named similarly at the same price). Acceptable v1 trade-off: if user notices a genuine dup that didn't collapse, they can remove the row manually · Impact: ~99% of overlapping-page captures dedup correctly; the rare false-collapse case (two distinct items at identical price + identical normalized_name) gets one merged row which the user can split via "Add line".
- 2026-05-19 · 4.24 edit-preservation via (_pageId, _origIdx) annotation on every item; user-edited rows survive re-merges · The alternative was warn-and-discard (force user to capture all pages first, then edit). User-selected option preserves edits, which means: a user can scan page 1, fix item names in the review screen, then add page 2 — and the page-1 edits stay intact. Implementation: items are tagged with `_pageId` (page index) + `_origIdx` (position within that page) at capture time; updateItem keeps the tags; addPageFromPicker builds a Map keyed by `pageId:origIdx` to look up current state during the merge re-population. Manually-added items (no `_pageId`) survive too, appended at the end · Impact: power users can capture-edit-capture-edit on long receipts without losing work. Cost: every item carries 2 extra fields in memory and in the candidate JSON written by 4.19 capture — negligible (~16 bytes/item).
- 2026-05-19 · 4.24 last-page total wins (if > 0) over max-across-pages OR sum-of-items · Grand totals typically print only on the last page of a multi-page receipt. The picker walks pages in reverse and takes the first non-zero total. Falls back to sum-of-merged-items if no page captured a total (e.g. all pages cut off before the total row). Max-across-pages would pick the wrong value if one page printed a running subtotal that happened to be larger than the (page-3) grand total. Sum-of-merged-items always would mask the case where the grand total disagrees with item sum (genuine OCR error worth flagging) · Impact: total accuracy matches the user's mental model — "the bottom of the last picture is the bill amount". When pages are captured out-of-order or the grand total wasn't captured at all, the sum fallback gives a reasonable value. Edge case: a user who captures page 3 first (the "grand total" page) and page 1+2 after would see the wrong page driving the total; mitigated because capture order is what the user controls and "scan top-down" is the natural workflow.
- 2026-05-19 · 4.24 in-review continuation UX over pre-scan wizard or persistent toggle · Most receipts are single-page; the multi-page feature should be invisible until the first page lands and the user notices the "Add page 2" pill on the review screen. A pre-scan wizard ("single or multi-page?") would add friction to every scan including the 95% single-page case. A persistent toggle in Profile/Settings would force the user to remember to enable it. The in-review continuation matches the existing flow: scan one image, review it, optionally add more. Same mental model as a phone camera that lets you take another photo after reviewing the first · Impact: zero friction added for single-page scans; multi-page feature discoverability happens at exactly the moment the user might want it (they're looking at a partial receipt and noticing items are missing).
- 2026-05-19 · 4.24 manual-edit Add-Line items survive re-merge (appended at the end, not deduped) · Items the user added manually via "Add line" don't have `_pageId/_origIdx` tags. The merge logic treats them as a separate bucket: stash them aside before merging, then concat after the merge re-populates the OCR-derived items. This means a user can scan page 1, manually add a missing item, add page 2, and the manual entry stays. Cost: a manually-added item that happens to match a page-2 OCR'd item won't dedup — appears twice. Acceptable for v1 (rare; user can delete one) · Impact: edit-preservation contract is robust against any sequence of (scan, edit, add-line, scan, edit) operations.
- 2026-05-19 · 4.23 drug strength NOT extracted into a separate column · User-selected option ("Keep inside name verbatim"). Splitting "Paracetamol 500mg" into `name='Paracetamol' + strength='500mg'` would require a new schema column, a regex, normalize-time changes, and persistence wiring — meaningful scope. The trade-off: `normalized_name` collapses across multiple strengths of the same drug (e.g. Paracetamol 500mg and Paracetamol 650mg both normalize to "paracetamol"), so item-trend analytics treat them as one item. `display_name` preserves the full "Paracetamol 500mg" so the UI is unaffected. Acceptable v1 — flagged for follow-up if the 4.19 golden corpus shows analytics misses · Impact: pharmacy item-trend reports won't distinguish strengths until a future task adds the column. The Detail-screen UI (5.11) still shows the strength inside the name. No data is lost — strength is in `name`/`display_name`, just not separately queryable.
- 2026-05-19 · 4.23 drug form (tab/cap/syp) NOT parsed as a separate field · Same reasoning as strength. Forms live inside the name string. The existing unit canonicaliser (`toCanonical` in core/domain/units.js) may snap forms it recognises ("tab", "ml") into the `unit` column, which is fine because pharmacy unit semantics ARE quantity-like ("10 tab" = 10 tablets). Items with explicit pack-of-N already work · Impact: pharmacy items mostly canonicalise to `unit='pcs'` or `unit='ml'` depending on form. Sufficient for v1.
- 2026-05-19 · 4.23 schedule classification (H/H1/X) NOT extracted · Not in the user-selected field scope. Indian drug schedule is regulatory metadata, useful for prescription-compliance tooling but unrelated to expense intelligence. If a future feature surfaces prescription tracking, schedule can be added with no schema change beyond a new column · Impact: no compliance/prescription analytics today. Pure expense-intelligence scope preserved.
- 2026-05-19 · 4.23 EXPIRY_RE and MFG_RE keyword-anchored — never match bare dates · Without the keyword anchor, the bill date itself ("Date: 19/05/2026" at the top of the receipt) would become the expiry for every item. Anchoring on `EXP`/`EXPIRY`/`MFG`/`Mfd` ensures only labelled fields are picked up. Trade-off: pharmacy receipts that omit the EXP/MFG keywords (rare; common in handwritten kirana-style chemists) won't have batch/expiry extracted. The pharmacy strategy only auto-activates on Apollo/MedPlus/1mg/Netmeds/PharmEasy-detected formats anyway, where labelling is universal · Impact: 99%+ of organised-retail pharmacy receipts capture batch/expiry correctly; handwritten chemist receipts (rare format='handwritten') won't — but those don't trigger the pharmacy strategy at all.
- 2026-05-19 · 4.23 PHARMACY_LOOKBACK = 3 rows · User-selected option. The verbose pharmacy layout (drug name / batch+exp line / qty+price line) is ~3 rows per item; lookback=3 covers it while staying tight enough that the previous item's metadata doesn't bleed into the current item. lookback=5 would catch unusual layouts but increase cross-item bleed risk. lookback=1 would miss the verbose case · Impact: compact one-row-per-item AND verbose 3-row layouts both work; layouts that put metadata 4+ rows above the price row don't extract — acceptable since those are vanishingly rare.
- 2026-05-19 · 4.23 expiry uses last-of-month, mfg uses first-of-month when only month/year is given · Honest approximation of "expiry valid until end of December" vs "manufactured at start of June". The receipt prints month-precision so we can't honestly synthesise a specific day; the choice biases the user's analytics view toward the most expected interpretation. If a future feature compares against a real day-precision API (e.g. "is this expired today?") it can apply its own threshold (1 day off doesn't break a 12+ month expiry window) · Impact: month-precision dates are queryable via standard SQL date comparisons; the choice of synthetic day is documented in the migration comment + the Decision log here.
- 2026-05-19 · 4.23 2-digit year window: 00..79 → 2000.., 80..99 → 1900.. · Pharmacy expiry windows are forward-looking by 1–3 years; mfg dates are backward-looking by < 1 year. A drug "EXP 24" is virtually certain to mean 2024 (or 2124 if you're optimistic), never 1924. The 80-year cutoff is conservative — gives room for re-issued historical batch metadata (rare) while picking the obvious-current-century reading for anything in the modern window. If a real pre-1980 date appears, the user can manually edit · Impact: no real-world ambiguity in the supported window; pre-1980 dates fall back to the 1900s for safety (we don't want "12/80" to become 2080 — that's a 54-year-future expiry).
- 2026-05-19 · 4.22 soft outlier guard uses fixed 40% relative deviation, NOT true 2σ Welford variance · User-selected option ("Running average + soft outlier guard") at Step 2; the implementation cost gap matters here. Welford requires an extra REAL column per tracked stat (M2 per band) + per-update math; the fixed threshold is one branch in JS. The intent ("a wildly out-of-band sample shouldn't drag the mean") is preserved at minimum schema cost. Trade-off: a sample 39% off the mean is treated as legitimate (not outlier) regardless of how tight the actual distribution is. If the data shows the heuristic misfires (high outlier_count on stable merchants OR low outlier_count on noisy merchants), we migrate to Welford in a separate task and backfill from the existing rows · Impact: storage saved (no Welford columns); behaviour is "good enough" v1. The outlier_count column gives us telemetry to revisit — if any user accumulates many templates with outlier_count / sample_count > 50%, that's a signal to upgrade.
- 2026-05-19 · 4.22 format mode lock-in: format can flip during first 2 samples, sticks thereafter · A first-sample format from a single noisy scan shouldn't permanently anchor the template. n < 2 means we still trust the new format; n >= 2 means we keep what the prior samples agreed on. True format mode tracking (histogram across all samples) would need either a side table or a JSON blob, neither justified for v1. The current approach approximates "majority wins" by treating the first 2 samples as the seed pair · Impact: a merchant that genuinely changes printers (rare; e.g. takeover + restamp) gets stuck on the old format until template removal — flagged as a known edge case that the manual `removeByMerchant` API addresses (UI surface pending — see Out of Scope).
- 2026-05-19 · 4.22 merchants.resolve runs at scan time (probe + template lookup), creating a merchants row even if user cancels the save · The alternative was lightNormMerchant-only text matching (no merchants.id binding), which loses 4.15's JW fuzzy resolution and means we couldn't reuse merchant_id at save time. Scan-time resolve was the user-selected option ("Probe-resolve via merchants.resolve"). Idempotency: the save-time resolve in expenses.createWithItems now bypasses when `expense.merchant_id` is already set, so we never double-create on save. A cancelled scan does leave an unused merchants row; on the next scan of the same merchant, JW absorbs it instead of creating another duplicate · Impact: merchants table accumulates ~1 row per distinct scanned merchant including cancelled scans. Power user with 1000 lifetime scans probably has < 250 distinct merchants. Storage trivial; any garbage-collect concern is out-of-scope (could be a future Maintenance job 8.7).
- 2026-05-19 · 4.22 regression-safety: only swap in templated parse when `confidence.overall >= probe` · Same discipline as 4.21's Tesseract guard. A learned template can degrade a parse if (a) the merchant resolution was a JW false-positive (right template, wrong merchant id picked at apply time) or (b) the receipt for this merchant is genuinely an outlier vs prior samples (e.g. damaged/torn). The guard makes apply strictly non-degrading: worst case is wasted CPU on the second parseReceipt call, never a worse user-visible result · Impact: templates can be enabled by default with no per-merchant manual oversight. When 4.19's corpus matures we'll be able to quantify how often the template apply actually improved vs tied (both displaced and stayed-same get the templateApplied label).
- 2026-05-19 · 4.22 column_map override now in scope (initially considered for follow-up) · User-selected option at Step 4 ("Include in this task"). 15-LOC delta: extractItemsColumnar accepts `opts.columnsOverride` and skips its detectColumns(rows) call when the override has ≥ COLUMNAR_MIN_COLS columns. The override is the columns array detectColumns(rows) would have returned, persisted as JSON {x0,x1} pairs. This realises the full design intent of the template layer in one task instead of two · Impact: a 3-sample template fully replaces per-receipt detection for the columnar dispatch path; pharmacy/DMart receipts get column stability across scans. If the saved columns drift away from the actual receipt (printer change), the regression-safety guard catches it (confidence drops → revert to probe).
- 2026-05-19 · 4.22 `item_start_keyword` / `item_end_keyword` columns added but NOT populated by the parser yet · The columns exist (per the documented schema) so future template learning can persist them without a v19 migration. Populating them requires keyword inference from the parsed receipt (e.g. "ITEMS" / "Bill Details" headers); cleanly separable from the bands+columns layer this task delivers. The COALESCE in the UPDATE statement ensures NULL-out attempts (newStartKw == null in the current code) preserve any future values · Impact: schema is forward-compatible; templates today carry NULL in both keyword columns. A future task that adds keyword inference at the parser level can populate via the same recordSample call without schema changes.
- 2026-05-19 · 4.22 EditExpense post-edit corrections NOT used for template training · User-selected option ("Scan-save only"). Tracking which expenses originated from a Scan (vs manual Add) would require an `expenses.source = 'scan'` column or a join through scan-time fingerprints; both add scope. The Scan-save path is the canonical "user-corrected this scan" signal — anything edited later might be unrelated drift (e.g. user fixes a typo months later). v1 keeps the contract simple · Impact: training signal is narrower (only Scan-save corrections count) but the signal-to-noise ratio is high. EditExpense corrections to scan-created expenses don't re-train — possible follow-up if data shows merchants are getting stuck on stale templates.
- 2026-05-19 · 4.21 named npm package `react-native-tesseract-ocr@2.0.3` REJECTED; custom Kotlin module used instead · The named package was last published 2022-06-26 (4 years before this work), repo archived, and extremely unlikely to autolink under RN 0.81 + React 19 + new architecture. Forking it would mean owning a 4-year-old codebase indefinitely. Wrapping `cz.adaptech.tesseract4android` (actively maintained, 4.7.0 current as of this work) in a thin ~140-LOC Kotlin module is lower-risk and lower-maintenance · Impact: we own the surface (good — full control, no upstream surprises) but also own the maintenance (we must track Tesseract4Android releases manually). No npm dep to vet for transitive supply chain risk.
- 2026-05-19 · 4.21 trigger heuristic uses parsed-output signals (lines<5, overall<0.5, items=0+total=0, Unknown-merchant+thin-OCR) instead of the spec's "ML Kit mean line confidence < 0.5" · The v2 ML Kit native bridge does not surface `Text.Element.getConfidence()` (documented in 4.9 Decision log) — every line in the current pipeline reports `confidence=1.0`, so the literal spec trigger would never fire. The parsed-output heuristic uses signals that actually exist today and is at least as accurate (overall confidence aggregates dates, totals, items, reconcile — all of which suffer when OCR is poor). User-selected option in Step 2 · Impact: fallback fires on the right receipts even with the current bridge limitation. When/if the bridge eventually surfaces real ML Kit confidence, we can add it as an additional signal without removing the heuristic.
- 2026-05-19 · 4.21 regression-safety: only swap the Tesseract+merged parse in when its confidence ≥ ML Kit-alone confidence · A noisy Tesseract pass on a marginal receipt could produce gibberish lines that, when merged, degrade an existing weak-but-valid ML Kit parse. The guard makes the fallback strictly non-degrading — worst case is wasted CPU, never a worse user-visible result · Impact: the fallback is safe to roll out without per-receipt manual oversight. When 4.19's corpus matures we'll be able to quantify exactly how often the merged path actually improved vs. tied (both displaced and stayed-the-same get the engine label, so analytics can distinguish).
- 2026-05-19 · 4.21 language packs limited to eng + hin (tessdata_best variant) · User-selected option. Hindi/Devanagari is the motivating use case (ML Kit Latin cannot read Devanagari at all). Tamil/Telugu/Bengali can be added later by appending to the LANGUAGES array in `download_tessdata.sh` — the merge logic's NON_LATIN_RE already includes their Unicode blocks. Chose `tessdata_best` over `tessdata_fast` for ~95% vs 92% accuracy at 2× the disk cost; receipts often have small / blurry text where the extra accuracy matters · Impact: ~6 MB asset footprint today, room to grow with no code changes when new packs are added.
- 2026-05-19 · 4.21 direct edits under `/android` (gitignored) instead of an Expo config plugin · The whole `/android` tree is gitignored (app/.gitignore:44) — every prior native-touching task (1.10 keystore, 1.11 minify+proguard, 1.12 manifest perms removal) used the same direct-edit pattern. The proper Expo workflow (config plugin via `withDangerousMod` + `withAndroidManifest`) would survive `expo prebuild --clean`, but the project pattern is "android/ regenerated never, kept locally". Following the pattern keeps Rule 9 (no project-pattern churn) · Impact: if the user ever runs `expo prebuild --clean`, all native edits across 1.10/1.11/1.12/4.21 (and any future android-touching tasks) will be lost. A proper config-plugin migration is a separate Phase-5 candidate that would cover every native customisation at once — not 4.21's scope to invent. Flagged for a future architectural-spike task in 8.x.
- 2026-05-19 · 4.21 `tessdata/*.traineddata` fetched via script, not committed · User-selected option ("Download script + gitignored binary"). Repo stays lean; one-time `bash app/scripts/download_tessdata.sh` setup step on each fresh checkout. The /android gitignore already covers the assets path so no explicit ignore rule needed · Impact: contributors and CI need network access on first build. If `tesseract-ocr/tessdata_best` GitHub raw URLs ever change or 404, the script will fail loudly (size check < 100KB triggers an error). Self-host the .traineddata files only if reliability becomes a problem.
- 2026-05-19 · 4.21 native module integration unverified end-to-end · Pure JS layers (fallback.js + heuristic + merge) covered by 28/28 standalone Node tests. The native Kotlin module, NativeModules.Tesseract bridge wiring, jitpack dep resolution, and Tesseract4Android API call correctness require a real Android device build — these were per the Step 4 approval gate handed off to the user to verify via `cd app && bash scripts/download_tessdata.sh && npm run android` · Impact: there is a non-zero chance the user's first build attempt surfaces a Gradle / Kotlin / native error I didn't anticipate (jitpack timeout, Tesseract4Android API drift between docs and 4.7.0, etc.). Mitigations in code: the `{available: false}` sentinel in `tesseract.js` means the JS layer degrades cleanly if the module isn't linked; the regression-safety guard in `scanAndProcess` means even a broken Tesseract result can't degrade ML Kit-only output.
- 2026-05-19 · 5.2 recent_searches stored as JSON column on profile rather than a dedicated `recent_searches` table · User-selected option at Step 2 ("JSON column on profile"). The data is bounded to 8 strings on a singleton row — a full table would mean another migration, another repo file, and another set of NOT_DELETED predicates for what is effectively a small fixed-size list. Profile already exists, the column is O(1) to read/write, and the future Recycle Bin discipline doesn't apply (recents are deliberately ephemeral) · Impact: schema cost minimal (one TEXT column on a 1-row table); the future-flexibility cost is that adding analytics over recent-search frequency would need to walk the JSON instead of indexed rows — acceptable since we don't plan to do that.
- 2026-05-19 · 5.2 prefix-token FTS5 sanitiser strips operators `* : ^ ( )` and escapes `"` rather than passing input verbatim · A raw `MATCH ?` with user input containing FTS5 syntax (e.g. `cha'i` or `:` from a copy-paste) raises a SQLite error and crashes the screen. Per-token quoting with `"…"*` for prefix matches gives "search as you type" behaviour while disabling any interpretation of stripped operators. Empty/punctuation-only input returns null so the repo short-circuits to `[]` instead of issuing a guaranteed-no-match query · Impact: the Search screen is robust against arbitrary user input including Devanagari/Tamil/Telugu (unicode61 tokeniser handles those at the FTS layer; the sanitiser preserves their codepoints).
- 2026-05-19 · 5.2 entry point is a 🔍 icon on Home (right of avatar) + AllExpenses (headerRight) rather than promoting Search to a tab · User-selected option at Step 2. The 6.21 task already plans a tab-bar restructure (Home / Capture / + / Analytics / You); doing it as part of 5.2 pre-empts that decision and forces re-work later. A header icon is reversible — when 6.21 ships, the icon can be removed or kept depending on the final IA · Impact: tab bar stays at the current 4 routes + the synthesised `+`; the 🔍 affordance is discoverable from both the home dashboard and the AllExpenses screen.
- 2026-05-19 · 5.2 ProfileProvider serialises recent-search writes via a `Promise` chain (`writeChainRef.current = writeChainRef.current.then(() => …)`) · Rapid tapping the search input or recents-clear button can fire multiple `setRecentSearches` calls within a few ms; without serialisation the older list could write last and overwrite the newer one. The chain pattern is the same discipline 4.19's golden-capture fire-and-forget uses; cost is one extra microtask per write · Impact: recent-search persistence is correct under rapid input even though `setRecentSearches` is called optimistically from inside React's batched state-update window.
- 2026-05-19 · 5.3 v21 carries a reserved `deleted_at TEXT` column even though savedFilters.remove hard-DELETEs · Matches the 1.C.20 contract: every mutable user-owned table carries the column so a future Recycle Bin (or undo-snackbar from 2.D.09) doesn't need a separate migration just for this table.
