The repo today filters every list through `deleted_at IS NULL`, so flipping to soft-delete is a one-line change in `remove()` later · Impact: schema is forward-compatible; the partial index `idx_saved_filters_updated WHERE deleted_at IS NULL` is the right cover for both modes.
- 2026-05-19 · 5.3 paymentMethods key allowlisted in CRITERIA_KEYS but builder no-ops it · 5.4 ships the column shortly. Allowlisting today means the criteria SHAPE is stable (FilterSheet, saved_filters, search criteria all carry the key); 5.4 lands as a small additive change (one frag in `buildWhere` + UI in FilterSheet) instead of a coordinated multi-file rewrite. Old saved_filters rows that pre-date 5.4 simply have no paymentMethods key — `normalizeCriteria` drops missing keys silently · Impact: 5.3 commits a forward-compatible criteria shape; 5.4 becomes UI-only on top of it.
- 2026-05-19 · 5.3 hand-rolled `Modal` for the bottom sheet rather than `@gorhom/bottom-sheet` or `react-native-modal` · User-selected option at Step 2 ("Hand-rolled modal sheet"). Drift already uses `presentation: 'modal'` Stack.Screen pattern for Add; a hand-rolled `Modal` with a Pressable backdrop, drag-handle visual, and content ScrollView matches that aesthetic and adds zero deps. @gorhom/bottom-sheet has nicer gesture/snap behaviour but introduces a non-trivial dep + native module surface; not justified for a single sheet in v1 · Impact: ~30 fewer KB in the bundle, no new native module; the sheet's height-cap at 88% viewport is enforced manually rather than via snap points (acceptable — there's only one snap state).
- 2026-05-19 · 5.3 saved-filter apply doesn't auto-close the FilterSheet · Tapping a saved-filter pill loads its criteria into the form state but stays open. Rationale: the saved filter is a starting point — users often need to tweak one axis before applying. Closing immediately would force a second open/edit cycle. The "Apply (N)" footer button is the explicit commit · Impact: saved filter is a template not a shortcut. The match-count chip immediately reflects the loaded criteria, so the visual feedback loop is intact.
- 2026-05-19 · 5.3 deferred Account and Trip dimensions from FilterSheet v1 · Both columns exist (3.5 added account_id / trip_id) and the builder allowlists their keys, so the data layer is ready. The UI surface was intentionally trimmed in the AskUserQuestion plan — Account requires merging with the Accounts feature for label lookups, Trip with Travel; both are real but not load-bearing for the core "find an expense" workflow that 5.2/5.3 unblocks. Adding them later is a UI-only follow-up (one section per axis in FilterSheet) · Impact: shipped surface is intentionally smaller; accountIds/tripIds in saved_filters criteria from external callers (or future imports) work today even though the v1 UI doesn't surface them.
- 2026-05-19 · 5.3 AllExpenses switches between in-memory feed and SQL feed based on `hasActiveFilters(criteria)` · Bypassing the in-memory `expenses` slice when filters are active means we don't have to filter a possibly-truncated 500-row in-memory list — SQL hits the real index. When criteria is empty we keep using the in-memory slice so the screen stays fast on rapid scrolling. The `sqlRows = null` sentinel cleanly toggles the two paths · Impact: filtered views can show > 500 rows (subject to the new `limit: 500` on the SQL call); pull-to-refresh re-fetches whichever feed is active. A future task can drop the in-memory branch entirely once 8.1 ships virtualisation, but Rule 9 discipline keeps the legacy path alive for now.
- 2026-05-19 · 5.3 `list()` carries both `legacyArgsToCriteria` translator and the new `criteria` arg in parallel · The translator means `expRepo.list({ categoryId, month })` from existing call sites (ExpensesProvider.refresh, AllExpenses route param) continues to use the indexed `month_key` path through buildWhere without those call sites needing edits. Rule 9: no churn outside the task. Adding `criteria` as a separate kwarg means new call sites use the explicit object; the two paths share one WHERE-builder so they can't drift · Impact: zero change to existing call sites; the future flip to criteria-only `list()` is a one-pass rename that doesn't touch business logic.
- 2026-05-19 · 5.3 saved_filters criteria stored as raw JSON.stringify rather than shredded columns · Single-user app, no cross-row analytics over filter contents, no expectation of querying "all saved filters that filter on Groceries". One TEXT column is simpler than 10 nullable typed columns + a many-to-many for moods/IDs, and forward-compatible with arbitrary criteria-shape evolution (5.4 paymentMethods, 7.3 tags, etc.) · Impact: criteria parsing happens at read time in the repo; round-trip through `normalizeCriteria` enforces the allowlist so unknown keys can't survive a save→load cycle.
- 2026-05-19 · 5.4 payment-method enum collapsed Credit + Debit → Card (5 values, not 6) · F-03 lists Credit/Debit separately but the analytics value of the split is marginal (both are bank-card; EMI is the meaningful credit-card sub-category, tracked separately by 7.5). Five chips fit one wrap line on small phones; six wrap. Lowercase canonical storage (`cash|upi|card|wallet|other`) means future relabel doesn't trigger a data migration · Impact: 5.4 ships with a tighter UX; users wanting Credit vs Debit can fall through to the `other` bucket with a note, or 5.4 can be revisited if usage demands the distinction.
- 2026-05-19 · 5.4 payment_method default seeded from "last expense with one set", falling back to `'upi'` (not `null` or `'cash'`) · Mirrors QW-17's last-category-used pattern; UPI is the dominant Indian retail payment method, so a clean-install default of `upi` produces correct data the majority of the time. Defaulting to `null` would require the user to tap the picker on every spend; defaulting to `cash` biases against the actual usage distribution · Impact: users still see and can change the picker, but the default minimises taps. Legacy expenses (pre-v22) stay NULL forever — the badge simply doesn't render on those rows.
- 2026-05-19 · 5.4 no index on payment_method · Five-value cardinality means an index would only pay off at 100k+ rows AND a single-value filter, and even then the index probe is followed by a full row read for the SELECT * surface. The filter axis composes through `buildWhere` so the (e.g.) `category_id IN + payment_method IN` query hits `idx_exp_cat_date` first and scans the (now-tiny) result · Impact: minimal write-path cost; no index maintenance. Reconsider when 5.8 batch ops or a future analytics surface filters on payment_method alone over a multi-year window.
- 2026-05-19 · 5.5 income kept as a separate table (not a `kind` column on expenses) · A `kind` column would have forced every existing list/rollup/FTS trigger to add a `kind='expense'` predicate, with high risk of missing a path. Separate tables mean: existing expense queries unchanged, soft-delete + month_key infrastructure cleanly reused via parallel index, no negative-amount-as-income overload (preserves SUM(amount) semantics on expenses), zero risk to the v12 monthly_summary rollup. Trade-off: cross-feature analytics (e.g. cashflowForecast) needs two SELECTs but composes cleanly with JS · Impact: 5.6 + future 6.x analytics that compare income vs expense run two queries instead of one — negligible at single-user scale.
- 2026-05-19 · 5.5 no `monthly_income_summary` rollup table · Following the same pattern as items pre-3.11 (no rollup until queries demanded it), income is small enough per month (one salary + a handful of variable entries) that the GROUP BY in `monthlyTotal` and `monthlyTrend` is bounded and cheap. Adding a rollup now means another trigger surface to maintain on every mutation · Impact: 5.6's widget reads a fresh SUM each refresh — still O(rows-in-month) = O(1) effectively. If 6.x analytics needs incremental income aggregates, a v24 migration can add the table without changing repo public APIs (internal SELECTs swap target).
- 2026-05-19 · 5.5 Income mode in Add is Quick-only (no Detailed/items form) · The Detailed mode exists to itemise expenses against pots — income doesn't have line items or pots, so reusing the form adds confusion. Keypad-only matches the "quick capture" Income use case (Salary → just type the amount, hit save) · Impact: itemised income tracking (e.g. tax-deductible source breakdown) isn't possible from Add. Defer to a dedicated EditIncome screen if/when needed.
- 2026-05-19 · 5.5 no FTS5 index on income, no Income on FilterSheet, no Income on Trends · 5.5's literal scope is "Income table + repo + Add toggle" plus 5.6's Home widget. Threading income through every analytics surface (search, filter, trends, scan, savings, export) is the next-iteration work. Decision-log entry exists so a future task can audit what's missing and pick up the slack · Impact: searching/filtering income in v1 is via list scan; the FilterSheet Search-Items toggle covers expenses + items only. Acceptable for v1 of income tracking.
- 2026-05-19 · 5.6 savings widget hidden entirely when `totalIncome === 0` · Showing "0% saved" or "Set income to see savings rate →" both add a row Home doesn't strictly need on the empty-state. Hiding cleanly until the user has at least one income entry preserves the Flow aesthetic — empty states stay empty · Impact: discovery of the Income mode happens through the Add toggle, not through a CTA on Home. If onboarding telemetry shows users miss the toggle, revisit with an empty-state nudge.
- 2026-05-19 · 5.6 over-spent state shows "Over by ₹X" instead of a negative percentage · Negative percentages on a rate widget read as broken / alarming. The savings rate floors at 0 (capped honestly so the math doesn't lie), and the prose flips from "₹X saved" to "Over by ₹X" with a coral rate-circle. Keeps the widget visually consistent regardless of sign · Impact: a user who has spent more than they earned this month sees coral framing without numeric overload; the bottom-of-Home "trending lighter" forecast widget still surfaces the budget-pace story.
- 2026-05-19 · 5.4 + 5.5 + 5.6 shipped in one batch · The three tasks are tightly coupled: 5.6 cannot ship without 5.5's income provider; 5.4 reserved a paymentMethods axis in 5.3's filters.js that needed wiring; 5.6 reads `totalIncome` from the IncomeProvider that 5.5 builds. Single PR keeps the migration-stamp sequence v22 → v23 contiguous and makes rollback semantics clean ("revert this commit drops both migrations + provider"). Validated by 88/88 assertions across 3 standalone scripts (filters pure-JS / SQLite ladder / savings math) · Impact: bigger single review surface; smaller migration count for the user's existing DB.
- 2026-05-19 · 5.4 + 5.5 — two migrations (v22 then v23), not combined · Per the standing one-task-one-migration rule (last opted-out by 3.1–3.10 only, with explicit decision-log entry). Keeps rollback triage per-feature. Negligible boot cost — two extra `schema_version` rows · Impact: future down-migration story (when 8.x backup/restore lands) treats each feature independently.
- 2026-05-19 · 4.20 deferred — no code · User-selected option in the kickoff AskUserQuestion. The justification for native preprocess (CLAHE / Sauvola / deskew) is improved OCR accuracy. Without 4.19's golden dataset (which 4.19 ships partial today, full when the user-export pipeline accumulates 50+ fixtures), there is no measurable signal to validate whether the preprocess actually moves accuracy. Implementing first would mean writing ~300 LoC of Kotlin + bridge code on faith, with no way to A/B the impact. Cost of deferring: the 4.16 fuel extractor is the last item-strategy improvement we can make without preprocess wins; further item-side gains will plateau until preprocessing improves OCR fidelity at the row level · Impact: 4.20 stays in the Phase 2.C queue. When 4.19 reaches the 50-fixture threshold and the harness establishes a baseline, 4.20 unblocks naturally. The decision is reviewable — if the user accumulates enough qualitative evidence that preprocess would help (e.g. many "needs-review" captures with the same blurry-image symptom), we can re-open the call.
- 2026-05-19 · 5.7 CSV format ships as ONE concatenated file with `# section: NAME` dividers, not three separate CSVs or a ZIP · expo-sharing's `shareAsync(uri)` takes a single URI; multi-file sharing needs a ZIP (= JSZip dep) or a sequence of share invocations (poor UX). The section-divider approach keeps one share-sheet dialog, opens in Excel as a single sheet where the dividers show as malformed rows (still readable), and is splittable by tooling on the `# section:` marker · Impact: power users who need pure-CSV-per-entity can either pick JSON (combined) or run a one-line split themselves. If the asymmetry becomes a complaint, a future task can add JSZip and switch to a `.zip` for CSV.
- 2026-05-19 · 5.7 PDF soft-cap at > 12 months (confirm Alert, not hard block) · Roadmap risk note says "Cap at 12 months per export." A hard block surprises users who have a legitimate reason to export longer ranges (annual review, hand-off to accountant); a confirm Alert preserves the warning while letting power users override. The risk is HTML→PDF performance, not data correctness · Impact: long-range PDFs may take seconds to render and produce large files; the confirm makes that user-driven, not a black-box surprise. CSV and JSON have no cap — they handle decades fine.
- 2026-05-19 · 5.7 batch-export hides Income toggle entirely · 5.8's selection mode is AllExpenses-only — there's no UI to select income rows. Forcing the toggle visible-but-locked would imply a feature that doesn't exist. Hiding it is the cleanest signal that the export is expense-scoped · Impact: a future "Batch select on Income screen" task (no leaf today) would need to surface its own ids → Export flow with the income toggle on. Architecturally clean — Export already conditions every entity on a presence-check.
- 2026-05-19 · 5.7 + 5.8 shipped in one batch · 5.8 batch-export reuses 5.7's generators verbatim — splitting into two PRs means a one-day landing of dead code (5.7) followed by another PR (5.8) that just adds a route.param hand-off. Bundling halves the migration-stamp churn (none in this case — pure feature work, no DB migration) and gives a single review surface for the criteria-axis change (ids) + the bulk repo methods + the screen wiring. Validated by 85/85 assertions across 3 scripts (pure-JS + node:sqlite) · Impact: bigger review surface, faster ship to the user. Rollback semantics: reverting this commit drops Export + selection mode + ids axis + bulk repo + listForExport methods together — no partial state.
- 2026-05-19 · 5.8 batch delete is HARD DELETE (parity with `expenses.remove()`), not soft-delete · User-selected option in the kickoff AskUserQuestion. The soft-delete columns (v2) + soft-delete-aware triggers/FTS exist for the eventual Recycle Bin feature, but that UI doesn't ship today. Soft-deleting in batch would create a class of rows the user couldn't see anywhere (vanish from every list, no Restore surface). Hard DELETE matches the single-row UX and stays consistent across the entire delete-path · Impact: the future Recycle Bin task (not currently a leaf) needs to flip BOTH `expenses.remove(id)` AND `expenses.bulkRemove(ids)` to soft-delete at the same time — flagged here so the migration doesn't miss the bulk path. Triggers already maintain rollups correctly on either delete mode.
- 2026-05-19 · 5.8 bulkRecategorizeExpenses calls full `refresh()`, not optimistic patch · `expenses.list` returns joined columns (`category_name`, `category_emoji`, `category_color`); recat-ing N rows in memory would require re-fetching the new category's join columns for each row anyway. A full SQL refresh after the bulk UPDATE is one indexed query that produces the correct shape immediately; a per-row patch would need a per-row repo.get() N times · Impact: a ~50ms full-refresh after batch recat (acceptable UX, RefreshControl already exists). Same applies to bulkRemove — the optimistic filter would work but the summary needs a recompute regardless, so the full refresh is simpler. If a future profiling pass shows the refresh is a bottleneck at 500 rows, the optimistic path can be added then.
- 2026-05-19 · 5.9 + 5.10 shipped in one batch with one migration (v24) · 5.9's autocomplete + auto-cat depend on 5.10's alias table + bundled JSON; splitting would mean a one-day landing of 5.10 schema with no UI consumer, followed by a 5.9 PR that wires it all up. Single PR keeps the migration-stamp sequence contiguous (v23 → v24, one new schema_version row) and gives a unified review surface. Validated by 80/80 assertions across 3 scripts · Impact: bigger single review surface; cleaner rollback semantics (reverting drops both features together).
- 2026-05-19 · 5.10 ships 58 seed merchants now, defers full ~1k corpus · User-selected option at Step 2. The full 1k corpus is fundamentally a data-curation task — categorising 1k Indian merchants requires judgement on ambiguous cases (Swiggy Instamart = food or groceries; BookMyShow = Fun or Subscriptions; Reliance Digital ≠ Reliance Fresh). Curating that without the user-correction loop in production produces a corpus that mis-categorises ~10-15% of edge cases until users override. Shipping the alias table + lookup pipeline + 58 well-chosen seeds gets the feature to users today; the next 950 entries become a data-fill task that doesn't need code review · Impact: bundled coverage is narrow but high-precision; the user-source rows in merchant_aliases learn the user's actual usage over time, so a smaller seed + an active learning loop converges faster than a big-seed + no learning.
- 2026-05-19 · 5.10 alias_key normalised via `lightNormMerchant` (matches `merchants.canonical_name`) · Same canonical form means a user typing "Swiggy Pvt Ltd" hits the same alias as a Scan capturing "Swiggy" — both canonicalise to "swiggy". Picking a different normalisation here would create two parallel learning paths that never converge. Using `merchants.canonical_name` directly was rejected because not every alias needs a matching `merchants` row (bundle seeds don't pre-create rows; legacy expenses with NULL merchant_id still surface aliases for their merchant text on edit) · Impact: alias matching is consistent end-to-end. Future change to `lightNormMerchant` (e.g. adding Hindi suffix strips) automatically applies to both layers — flagged for testing if that change ever happens.
- 2026-05-19 · 5.10 partial UNIQUE index on `(alias_key, source)` includes only `WHERE deleted_at IS NULL` · This lets `clearUserAliases` tombstone every user row without violating the constraint on a subsequent fresh user choice. SQLite supports partial indexes for UNIQUE constraints since 3.8; tested via the v24 ladder + soft-delete-then-reinsert scenario in the validation harness. Trade-off: counting tombstones requires a separate non-partial query — acceptable since the only thing that cares is a hypothetical analytics surface that doesn't exist yet · Impact: forward-compatible with the eventual Recycle Bin pattern (3.x soft-delete contract); the partial index keeps live-row writes cheap.
- 2026-05-19 · 5.10 bundle entries are NOT pre-inserted into merchant_aliases at install time · The bundle JSON lives in the JS bundle; pre-inserting all 58 rows on first boot (or migration apply) would mean: (a) the bundle is duplicated in storage (JSON + DB rows), (b) any future bundle edit triggers a migration to refresh seeded rows, (c) a user override would need to either delete the bundle row or carry a source='bundle-overridden' state. Reading the JSON at lookup time and only writing source='user' rows on a confirmed user save keeps the layers clean: bundle is read-only, user-source is the only mutable surface · Impact: the bundle can evolve with each app release just by editing the JSON; no migration churn. The cost is one JSON parse on lookup (sub-ms — happens at app start once, then cached as a module-level constant).
- 2026-05-19 · 5.9 MerchantDetail mirrors ItemTrend's UX shape (hero + 12mo bar + breakdown + recents) · User-selected option at Step 2 ("Trend + recent + categories"). Reusing ItemTrend's structural pattern means: users moving between item-detail and merchant-detail see the same affordances in the same places; future shared-component refactors (e.g. a `TimelineBarChart` primitive) can serve both surfaces; the "Last bought: ₹X" → "Last visited: ₹X · all-time avg ₹Y" framing translates 1:1 · Impact: zero shared UI code today (intentional — Rule 9), but the structural parallel makes a future extraction trivial when 8.x SectionList virtualisation lands and a shared primitive starts paying off.
- 2026-05-19 · 5.9 silent auto-cat over suggestion-chip-with-undo-toast · User-selected option at Step 2. The silent auto-switch is invisible 90% of the time (correct guess; user doesn't notice) and unobtrusive even on a wrong guess (user just taps a different pill — and that tap locks the manual choice for the session via `userTouchedCategoryRef`). A suggestion chip would add visual chrome to every Add session, including the majority where there's no alias hit. An undo toast would create a 3-second persistent overlay on a screen the user typically dismisses in under 5 seconds · Impact: most users never see the auto-cat fire because it's correct; misfires are recoverable in one tap with the user's correction reinforcing the alias for next time. Lower-friction than alternatives; tighter learning loop.
- 2026-05-19 · 5.9 dropdown over recents-chips for autocomplete · User-selected option at Step 2. Dynamic dropdown gives "search as you type" behaviour matching what users expect from any modern merchant input (Google Maps, Swiggy itself); recents-chips would require the user to know which 3 merchants Drift considers "recent" before typing. Combining both would clutter a screen Drift deliberately keeps minimal · Impact: typing is required (one-tap shortcut from recents not available); but the bundle's silent auto-cat covers the "no-typing" path for any merchant in the seed corpus. When 5.14 (predictive amount) ships, the existing "Last time ₹X" chip can sit alongside the dropdown without conflict.
- 2026-05-19 · 5.9 `merchants.suggest` uses JW threshold 0.78, but `merchants.resolve` keeps 0.92 · Suggestion is a "did you mean?" surface — surfacing near-misses to help the user pick the right merchant is preferable to silence. Resolve, in contrast, decides whether to MERGE the typed merchant into an existing row; the higher threshold is correct because a wrong merge corrupts merchant_id permanently. Two thresholds for two distinct semantic operations · Impact: typeahead may surface unrelated-looking matches at the bottom of the dropdown (e.g. "Mandi House" for "Mandi"), but the user picks the right one explicitly; no automatic merge happens. The validation harness explicitly tests the typo path ("zoomato" → zomato shown).
- 2026-05-19 · 5.9 `expenses.create()` and `update()` now resolve `merchant_id` (was Scan-only) · Reverses the 4.15 Decision-log entry that deferred this to 5.9 specifically because 5.9 is the consumer. Without this change, manual Add records leave `merchant_id` NULL forever and MerchantDetail/topMerchants misses them entirely — the feature works only for scan-created expenses · Impact: every new manual Add (including from EditExpense's merchant-text rename) gets linked. Existing rows with NULL merchant_id stay NULL until they're edited — a one-shot backfill migration could fix them but is left out per Rule 9 (no churn outside the explicit task). When the user next touches an old expense, update()'s re-resolve handles it.
- 2026-05-19 · 5.9 backfill of legacy NULL `expenses.merchant_id` deliberately NOT shipped · A SQL backfill that walks every expense, runs lightNormMerchant on the merchant string, and resolves against the merchants table would touch every existing row, populate merchant_id, and possibly create new merchants rows. Risk: a JW false-match merges legacy spends with unrelated merchants (the merchants table has no curation in v1). User-driven backfill via the update() path is safer — every NULL row gets resolved the next time the user opens it for any reason, with the user implicitly attesting the merchant text is correct · Impact: MerchantDetail/topMerchants under-count until users naturally touch their old records; a future "Rebuild merchant index" admin action could surface this if desired.
- 2026-05-19 · 5.9 `ExpensesProvider.addExpense/addExpenseWithItems` now return the saved row · Additive change — every existing caller discards the return value, so nothing breaks. Add.js needs the row to read its `merchant_id` for `aliases.recordUserChoice`. The alternative was Add.js calling the repo directly (bypassing the provider's optimistic patching + summary refresh), or doing a separate `expRepo.get` after the save (extra DB round-trip). Returning the row is the smallest correct change · Impact: future callers can rely on the return value for post-save chained work (e.g. immediate navigation to Detail with the new id). No-op for existing call sites.
- 2026-05-19 · 5.9 `userTouchedCategoryRef` resets when merchant input is cleared, but stays sticky across edits · Once a user manually taps a pot pill for THIS Add session, every subsequent merchant edit (typing, picking suggestion, blurring) skips the silent auto-cat. Reset triggers only on `merchant === ''`, which is the user explicitly starting over. This means a user who scans Swiggy, taps "Groceries" manually (overriding the bundle's Food guess), then re-types "Swiggy" still won't have Food re-suggested. They've made their intent clear · Impact: predictable behaviour — the user's manual choice always wins for the rest of the form session. The persistent learning happens at save time via `recordUserChoice`, not at category-pill-tap time.
- 2026-05-19 · 5.9 leaderboard accessible from Profile, not from a tab · Same reasoning as 5.2's Search-icon entry point: 6.21's tab restructure is the right time to decide whether Merchants gets a top-level surface. A Profile row is reversible — when 6.21 ships, the row can be removed or kept depending on the IA call. Meanwhile users have one entry point (Profile → Top merchants) plus the inline tap from Detail.js · Impact: tab bar stays at 4 routes + the synthesised `+`; Merchants discoverable via Profile and via merchant-tap from any expense detail. Sufficient for v1.
- 2026-05-19 · 5.9 `Merchants` leaderboard "All" preset uses 600-month proxy, not a separate code path · Picking "All" is rare on a personal app (the default 6mo window is the relevant view 95% of the time). A 600-month value (50 years) is functionally "all-time" given the use case, hits the same SQL path as the other presets, and saves a conditional in the repo. The validation harness confirms 12mo correctly includes the 10-month-old Swiggy row that 6mo excludes; "All" is just a larger window of the same query · Impact: one less code path to maintain; the literal "all-time" semantics is preserved at the limit of any realistic user dataset.
- 2026-05-19 · 5.12 legacy `receipt_uri` column kept populated alongside the new `receipt_path` / `receipt_thumb` / `receipt_bytes` · User-selected option at Step 2. The Detail screen's image-viewer (5.13) and the AllExpenses thumbnail badge (when it lands) both currently read `receipt_uri`. Flipping the readers would pull 5.13's viewer scope and 5.15's lazy-migrate scope into 5.12 — violates Rule 9 (no churn outside the task). Two-column overlap is cheap (~50 bytes per row); 5.15 is the explicit task that lazy-migrates legacy rows on first read AND swaps every reader · Impact: from now on, new scans write BOTH columns; existing rows still have only `receipt_uri` until 5.15 ships. 5.13's viewer can keep using `receipt_uri` and silently get the better path-quality once 5.15 cuts over.
- 2026-05-19 · 5.12 `expenses.create()` and `expenses.update()` deliberately NOT extended · Manual Add has no receipt UI today, and EditExpense's "attach receipt" affordance is also out of scope (would belong to 5.13 or 5.15). Extending the two methods now would mean dead code paths — every parameter null on every call until that affordance ships. Rule 9 — keep the touch surface tight · Impact: only the Scan path populates the new columns. The lazy-migrate in 5.15 (or a future "attach receipt to existing expense" feature) is responsible for any non-scan write of receipt_path/receipt_thumb/receipt_bytes.
- 2026-05-19 · 5.12 EXIF stripping via JPEG re-encode side effect (no explicit strip step) · `expo-image-manipulator.manipulateAsync` constructs new JPEG bytes from the decoded RGBA bitmap; the original EXIF block (camera make/model, GPS, timestamps) is not propagated into the output because the manipulator doesn't write EXIF — only image data. Documented and verifiable behaviour. An explicit strip would require an additional library (sharp, piexifjs) that doesn't run on React Native, or a native module. Foundation scope doesn't justify it · Impact: privacy guarantee for foundation-scope receipt storage. 8.6's complete pipeline can add explicit verification (or a sha-1 of the file bytes that doesn't depend on EXIF cleanliness) if defence-in-depth is wanted later.
- 2026-05-19 · 5.12 flat `full/` + `thumb/` subdirs, not partitioned yyyy/mm/ · User-selected option at Step 2. The complete pipeline (8.6) will move to `{full,thumb}/yyyy/mm/<uuid>.webp` with WebP + sha-1-based names — that's its own migration with its own file-move pass. Building the partitioned structure now means doing the work twice when 8.6 lands · Impact: foundation scope is tight; one-time file move when 8.6 ships will be batched with the WebP re-encode pass anyway.
- 2026-05-19 · 5.12 no image-byte hash column populated · v7's `receipt_hash` + `receipt_soft_hash` columns are taken — 4.14 reused them for the parsed-shape OCR fingerprint (duplicate-bill detection on scan). The image-byte sha-1 (true image dedup) is 8.6's responsibility. Adding a new column for image hash now would mean a migration just to support a column 5.12 has no consumer for · Impact: 5.12 can't dedup on raw image bytes (two scans of the same receipt with no OCR difference will write two `full/<uuid>.jpg` files). 8.6's sha-1 + filename-by-hash approach naturally fixes this. Until then, 4.14's OCR-content dedup catches the same-receipt-twice case as long as the parser produces the same shape.
- 2026-05-19 · 5.11 effective-rate display only when subtotal > 0 · A row labelled `CGST (2.5%)` is only meaningful when there's a positive subtotal to apply the rate to. When OCR-driven amounts collapse (amount ≤ Σtax, rare but possible for restaurants where the cgst/sgst totals were inflated), suppressing the rate suffix prevents misleading the user (e.g. "CGST (300%)" if subtotal=10 and cgst=30). The components themselves remain visible because they're the canonical data — just unannotated · Impact: when receipt data is sound the user sees the educational rate annotation; when it's not, they see clean numbers without a derived-and-suspicious-looking percentage.
- 2026-05-19 · 5.11 Tax-total row carries `emphasis: true` with a cream background · Visually anchors the breakdown — the row is the answer the user is looking for ("how much GST did I pay on this receipt?"). Same cream tone as the existing hero card so it reads as a related summary surface. Reused F.cream rather than introducing a new design token · Impact: zero new theming; the card already has the right palette in scope.
- 2026-05-19 · 5.11 per-item HSN/GST rates intentionally NOT surfaced in items list · User-selected option at Step 2. The items list is a high-frequency surface (every Detail visit) and adding rate chips would clutter the existing `name · qty/unit · unit_price · price` layout. HSN drill-down is a low-frequency need (almost no consumer surface needs it; it matters for business expense exports — which Export 5.7 already handles via CSV). When item-level tax intelligence becomes useful (Phase 3 analytics on tax-per-category, or a future GST-claim export), a dedicated screen or an expandable row is the right home · Impact: scope tight; no new UI complexity in the high-traffic Detail items list. Item-level GST rate data still persists (v17 columns) and is queryable via direct SQL whenever a consumer surface lands.
- 2026-05-19 · 5.11 + 5.12 shipped in one batch · Both touch the Scan/Detail axis (5.11 displays GST captured during scan; 5.12 persists the receipt image captured during scan) and neither requires a migration. Splitting would mean two near-identical task-tracker updates and two separate Babel parse-checks. The Decision log on each task stays distinct (5.11 = UI surface for already-persisted data; 5.12 = new persistence layer with new file IO) · Impact: smaller review surface per task, single tracker update, contiguous file edits. The implementation order (5.11 first, 5.12 second) is preserved in the Completion log for replay.

- 2026-05-19 · 5.8 selection cap at 500 ids, same-ref returned when blocked · SQLite's default `SQLITE_MAX_VARIABLE_NUMBER` is 999; the repo chunks at 500 to stay well under. Capping the UI at the same 500 makes the chunk behaviour invisible (one txn, one chunk). Returning the same Set ref on a cap-blocked toggle means React's useState bail-out skips the re-render — important when long-pressing the same already-blocked row repeatedly · Impact: a power user with > 500 expenses can't select them all at once; they hit the cap silently (no toast). Acceptable for v1; a "Select all in date range" pre-seed could lift this without changing the cap if usage demands it.

- 2026-05-19 · 5.13 + 5.14 + 5.15 shipped in one batch · All three touch the same Detail/Add/receipt axis: 5.13 builds the viewer that 5.15's lazy-migrate hook lives in, 5.15 supplies the receipt-URI selection helper that 5.13's thumb consumes, and 5.14 is a small chip wired into the same Add.js merchant trigger points already touched by 5.9/5.10. Splitting would mean three near-identical Babel parse-checks and three sequential validation runs against largely-overlapping touched files (Detail.js, Add.js, repo.js, filters.js). One bundled review preserves the dependency edge (viewer ↔ migrate) explicitly · Impact: smaller review surface; Phase 2.A closes in one tracker update; each task retains its own Decision-log entries below for replay.
- 2026-05-19 · 5.13 hand-rolled pinch/zoom over react-native-gesture-handler 2.28 + reanimated 4.1, not a new image-zoom library · User-selected option at Step 4. Both libraries are already installed and active in App.js (`GestureHandlerRootView` wraps the navigation root, so child `GestureDetector`s register cleanly even inside the Modal). A third-party library would add maintenance risk plus possible gesture-fight with the modal backdrop; the hand-rolled version is ~140 LOC with clear bounds (`scale ∈ [1, 4]`, snap-back below 1.05, pan-envelope clamp at `(s-1)*dim/2`, double-tap toggles 1× ↔ 2×). All RNGH/Reanimated imports lazy-required so a dev shell missing the native rebuild falls through to a static contain-fit Image (the rest of the UI still works) · Impact: zero new dependencies, fully on-device, matches Drift's deps-light norm. If a future task needs heavier image features (cropping, rotation, annotation), the lazy-require shape makes it easy to swap to a richer library without bleeding into the rest of the Detail screen.
- 2026-05-19 · 5.15 lazy migrate runs only on viewer open (not on app boot) · User-selected option at Step 2. App-boot sweeps would burn CPU + disk on every launch even when the user never opens an old receipt; user-driven migration means cost is bounded by attention. Failure mode is graceful: persistReceipt returns null when the cache URI is gone, the viewer keeps showing receipt_uri (potentially broken) with no DB write — no row gets stamped with a bogus path · Impact: legacy rows the user never revisits never get migrated. That's fine — they're already broken (cache evicted) and a re-attach affordance is a future feature. The expensive case ("user has 5000 old receipts and tries to migrate all at once via a Profile button") doesn't exist as a code path yet, so we don't pay for it.
- 2026-05-19 · 5.15 keep `receipt_uri` populated even after lazy migrate succeeds · The natural cleanup would be `UPDATE … SET receipt_uri = NULL` once `receipt_path` is set; the deliberate choice is to keep it. Two reasons: (1) safety net if a future expo upgrade shifts `documentDirectory`, the legacy column might be the only working URI for the cache slice (vanishingly unlikely but the cost of keeping the column is ~30 bytes/row); (2) the COALESCE(`receipt_path`, `receipt_uri`) reader pattern means there's nothing to gain by clearing it — both columns being populated produces the same render path as just `receipt_path` set · Impact: 30 bytes/row × 100k receipts ≈ 3 MB on a fully migrated DB. Acceptable; will revisit if storage becomes a constraint.
- 2026-05-20 · 2.D.11 explicit Edit link beside the cancel/reinstate link, whole-row tap preserved · User-selected option at Step 2 Q1. The pencil-icon alternative would have introduced a new visual element type that doesn't appear elsewhere on this screen; the header-hint alternative was a documentation patch rather than an affordance. The text-link approach reuses the small-link styling that's already in the row (the existing Cancel/Reinstate link was in the same slot), so the visual delta is minimal: one extra text + a `·` separator. Whole-row tap → Edit is preserved because (a) it's the most natural gesture and (b) discoverability-by-doing is still valuable even with a visible affordance · Impact: row right-stack adds ~30px of horizontal space; minor risk of the long sub-name column getting squeezed when both 'Mark cancelled' and 'Edit' render, mitigated by the row's existing `flex: 1` middle column.
- 2026-05-20 · 2.D.12 "Mark cancelled" not "Mark as cancelled (reminder only)" · User-selected option at Step 2 Q2. The exact tracker phrasing ("Mark as cancelled (reminder only)") is 28 characters, which would have wrapped to 3-4 lines in the small right-stack slot. The chosen short form keeps the row layout sane and the ambiguity is resolved by a separate one-line hint card above the first row. The hint is dismissible (session-only) so the user can opt out once they've internalised the model, but re-appears next launch as a gentle reminder — that re-reminder is a feature, not a bug, for a bookkeeping action where misclick costs minor user friction · Impact: language consistency across the screen (gentle-suggestion CTA also renamed); zero schema change; hint state lives only in React state, no persistence layer.
- 2026-05-20 · 2.D.13 client-side sort, not a repo-level ORDER BY · `subs.list()` continues to return rows in `created_at DESC, id DESC` order (subs.repo.js:6); the screen does a `useMemo`-cached client-side sort on top. Reason: subs counts are small (typically <100; even a power user with 200 active+cancelled subs is rare), so `Array.prototype.sort` is essentially free. Pushing sort into SQL would mean either (a) a parameterised ORDER BY in the repo (cross-cutting change for a UI-only feature), or (b) a separate `list({ sortBy })` method that the context picks up — bigger surface area, and the JS-side `monthlyEquivalent` calculation (yr ÷ 12, wk × 4.33) is awkward to express in SQLite without a CASE expression. Client-side sort is the right tool for the data volume · Impact: if subs count ever climbs into the thousands (unlikely on a personal app), a future optimisation can push sort into SQL with the existing repo signature unchanged.
- 2026-05-20 · 2.D.09 soft-delete with restore, not hard-delete + JS-replay · User-selected option at Step 2 Q1. The repo schema already had `deleted_at` columns on every mutable user-owned table (v2 + later) and v12 AU triggers on expenses + receipt_items that reconcile monthly_summary / item_summary / expense_fts / item_fts on the deleted_at flip in either direction — the infrastructure has been waiting for a consumer since 2026-04. Soft-delete keeps IDs stable (so a Detail screen open with the old id still resolves after Undo), avoids per-table JS payload capture for FK children, and the AU triggers do the rollup math automatically. The capture-and-replay alternative would have meant: collecting expense + receipt_items + goals/contributions etc. into JS state, re-INSERTing on Undo with new IDs (breaking any in-flight navigation), re-firing every AI trigger (which would have to net out cleanly). Cleaner to use the architecture that was already designed for this · Impact: future Recycle Bin consumes the same state with zero migration; restore is one indexed UPDATE per row.
- 2026-05-20 · 2.D.09 expenses.remove/restore cascade to receipt_items inside the same transaction · Discovered during validation: SQLite's `FK ON DELETE CASCADE` only fires on a real DELETE, not on UPDATE deleted_at. Without the app-level cascade, soft-deleting an expense left its receipt_items alive — item_summary continued to count them, and a future "tracked items" view would mis-report. The fix is a single extra UPDATE inside the same `withTransactionAsync` (`UPDATE receipt_items SET deleted_at = datetime('now') WHERE expense_id = ? AND deleted_at IS NULL`). Restore mirrors it. Bulk variants do the same with placeholders. The v12 receipt_items AU trigger fires per-row and correctly decrements item_summary for each child; the transaction means a partial cascade can't observe (all-or-nothing). Validated explicitly in /tmp/drift_2_D_09_validate.mjs (test "item_summary cascades to 0 when parent expense soft-deletes") · Impact: item_summary stays consistent with the parent expense's visibility. Future tasks that introduce parent-child cascades on other tables (e.g. categories → expenses) need to follow this pattern explicitly; SQLite doesn't get it for free.
- 2026-05-20 · 2.D.09 soft-deleted rows stay indefinitely, no automatic purge · User-selected option at Step 2 Q4. Storage cost is ~30 bytes per soft-deleted row × realistic delete rate ≈ negligible. The alternative ("promote to hard-delete after 5s") would have permanently destroyed the Recycle Bin substrate for any user who didn't tap Undo within 5s — which is most of the time. The 30-day background sweep alternative would have added a startup-time cost and a maintenance burden for a UX nobody has asked for yet. Indefinite retention is the right v1 default because it preserves all options for the next iteration (Recycle Bin, manual "Empty trash", date-based sweep — pick any) · Impact: the deleted_at column accumulates timestamps over time; a future Recycle Bin task can sort by deleted_at DESC and offer per-row purge. If storage ever becomes a concern, a one-shot sweep migration can purge anything older than N days without breaking any existing reader.
- 2026-05-20 · 2.D.09 every remove() uses `AND deleted_at IS NULL` guard · Without the guard, calling remove() on an already-deleted row would overwrite the original timestamp with a fresh one. The guard makes remove() idempotent — a double-tap on a row's Delete that races the first SQL UPDATE is a no-op rather than a timestamp-trampling mutation. Validated by /tmp/drift_2_D_09_validate.mjs (test "AND deleted_at IS NULL guard makes remove idempotent") with a 1.1s sleep between calls to ensure datetime('now') would actually differ on the second invocation · Impact: a future Recycle Bin sort-by-delete-date stays meaningful; restore() always returns the row to its original pre-delete state without subtly stamping a new "deleted at" history.
- 2026-05-20 · 2.D.09 Toast component extended with optional action, not a separate UndoSnackbar component · The existing Toast (QW-12 / 2.D.01) had the right substrate: Reanimated 4 slide-down animation, theme-aware F.ink + F.coral palette, timer-based dismissal with replace-in-place, mounted once at AppRoot inside AppProvider. Extending it (one extra prop bag) is ~25 LOC; building a separate UndoSnackbar would have meant duplicating the animation choreography + safe-area handling + timer management for a UI that's visually identical save for a tappable action button. The `pointerEvents` flip from 'none' to 'box-none' when an action is provided is the only material behavioural change — passive toasts still don't intercept taps · Impact: one component, one mental model. If a future task wants a third flavour (e.g. progress toast with cancel), the same pattern extends — add another opts key.
- 2026-05-20 · 2.D.09 Subs long-press menu Alert eliminated entirely · Pre-2.D.09 long-press on a sub opened an Alert with Edit / Delete / Cancel options. Edit is reachable by tapping the row (which navigates to EditSub). Delete is reachable via the new swipe action AND via the long-press itself (which now triggers the same delete-with-Undo flow). The third "Cancel" Alert button was just dismiss-the-menu. So the menu had no unique affordance — every option was reachable elsewhere. Replacing it with a direct delete keeps the long-press gesture useful (it's the discoverability fallback for users who don't try swipe) without re-introducing a destructive Alert · Impact: one fewer Alert surface; long-press semantics now match the 5.8 selection-mode entry pattern conceptually (long-press = primary action). If discoverability suffers we can revisit.
- 2026-05-20 · 2.D.07 ReanimatedSwipeable from RNGH 2.28, not a hand-rolled gesture composition · The 5.13 receipt viewer chose hand-rolled gestures (Decision log entry 654) because the gesture set was specific (pinch + double-tap-zoom + pan with bounds clamping) and a third-party image-zoom library would have been a maintenance liability. Swipe-to-delete is a much more standard pattern — `ReanimatedSwipeable` is the modern, maintained API in `react-native-gesture-handler` (the legacy `Swipeable` is deprecated as of 2.18; 2.28 still ships both). Using it gives us battle-tested snap-back semantics, threshold-based open detection, action panel rendering, and the close() method on a ref — ~50 LOC of wrapper vs ~120 LOC if hand-rolled, with zero new dependency surface (RNGH already pulled in by 5.13). The lazy-require pattern mirrors 5.13's fallback so a dev shell without native rebuild falls through to a plain View · Impact: gesture surface stays consistent with industry idiom; the wrapper API (`rightLabel/rightIcon/rightColor/onRightAction/enabled`) is general enough to host future swipe-to-cancel or swipe-to-archive flavours without forking the component.
- 2026-05-20 · 2.D.07 swipe-to-delete is hard DELETE with toast, no Undo button · User-selected option at Step 2 Q3. Hard delete matches the existing `expenses.remove`, `subs.remove`, and 5.8's `bulkRemove` semantics (Decision log entry 624) — keeping all single-row and bulk delete paths consistent means a future Recycle Bin flip touches exactly the same places. Adding an Undo button now would pre-empt 2.D.09's design space (which is the explicit "Undo snackbar on delete" task). The toast (QW-12 / 2.D.01 infrastructure, already mounted at AppRoot) is the confirmation affordance — silent enough to not interrupt flow, loud enough to make the action discoverable. The 25% drag-open threshold plus the explicit tap on the revealed action panel makes accidental delete unlikely · Impact: 2.D.09 remains the right place to introduce Undo + soft-delete. When that task lands, three single-row delete paths + 5.8's bulk path all need to flip in one batch — captured here so the migration scope is clear.
- 2026-05-20 · 2.D.07 receipt-item swipe-delete does NOT recompute parent expense amount · The natural-looking choice would be "after deleting one receipt item, set expense.amount = sum(remaining items)" to keep the receipt total consistent. The deliberate choice is to leave amount unchanged. Three reasons: (1) the parent expense's `amount` represents the receipt's actual paid total (Scan extracts it from the receipt's TOTAL field, which includes taxes/fees/discounts and isn't necessarily Σ items); (2) the Edit screen's "Break into items" toggle already offers an explicit recompute path for users who want sum-of-items to drive total; (3) recomputing here would cascade through `monthly_summary` triggers, `expense_fts` rebuild, possibly category rollups — a much bigger blast radius than this task targets. So the receipt items list is a view-only itemisation surface; removing an item is "I don't want this in my itemisation" not "this expense was actually cheaper" · Impact: Detail.js may show `amount: ₹420` with items summing to `₹350` after a swipe-delete. That's intentional — the truth is what the user paid (₹420 is on the receipt), the missing ₹70 is in taxes/fees the user didn't itemise. If users find this confusing in practice, a future task can add a small "items don't match total" note + a "Sync amount to items" CTA on the Detail card.
- 2026-05-20 · 2.D.07 row backgroundColor on AllExpenses + Detail items flipped from `'transparent'` to `F.surface` · ReanimatedSwipeable renders the action panel underneath the children container; when at rest the children container needs an opaque background or the red Delete panel bleeds through. The parent day-group container on AllExpenses (and the items-card container on Detail) is already `F.surface`, so the visual result is identical at rest — the change is invisible until you start swiping. Without this flip, users would see a faint red tint through the row even before touching it · Impact: no visual regression; the swipe reveal effect works cleanly. If a future task ever introduces row-level theming variants (e.g. zebra striping), the explicit background becomes the place to wire that.
- 2026-05-20 · 2.D.08 closed via `(= 5.8)` cross-reference, no new code · execution_roadmap.md L606 describes 5.8 as "Batch select mode on AllExpenses (long-press, action bar, batch recategorize, batch delete, batch export)". 2.D.08's leaf line ("Long-press to enter multi-select on AllExpenses") is precisely the gesture-entry side of that scope. `AllExpenses.js:319` has carried `onLongPress={() => toggleSelect(e.id)}` since 5.8 shipped on 2026-05-19. Re-implementing 2.D.08 would just be writing the same code twice. The user explicitly chose "Re-verify 2.D.08 scope before closing" at Step 2 Q4 — verification done · Impact: phase totals catch up by +2 (37 → 38/50) in one update without duplicate work. Cross-ref pattern matches the QW-12 / 2.D.01 dual-ID precedent.
- 2026-05-20 · 2.D.05 inline collapse instead of a settings.mood_tracking column · User-selected option at Step 2 Q1. The `carbon_tracking`-style precedent (Profile toggle gating a section) would have meant a new migration v17, a settings.repo extension, a Profile UI row, and a second engagement boundary (toggle on → engage per-entry) — too much surface area for a section that's already small. Inline collapse achieves "mandatory mood picker on every entry" elimination with three localised edits and zero schema delta. Future users who actively want mood-on-every-entry can still tap the chip; future users who never want it just never tap. If a "hide entirely" preference becomes a real demand, layering a settings flag on top is additive · Impact: the audit complaint from `execution_roadmap.md:124` ("mood picker mandatory on every entry") is resolved without locking us out of the toggle path later. Schema stays at v16 (current head); no migration risk.
- 2026-05-20 · 2.D.05 unset state stored as NULL, not silent 😌 · User-selected option at Step 2 Q3. The pre-2.D.05 implementation stored mood='😌' even for users who never opened the picker — that polluted any future "mood distribution" analysis (every user looks Worth-It-defaulted). NULL gives downstream readers a true tri-state: "user picked 😌" / "user picked something else" / "user didn't pick". `filters.js`'s mood IN-clause already gates on `c.moods.length > 0` (filters.js:157), so a NULL mood row simply doesn't satisfy any mood filter — semantically correct. Export drops to empty string (export.js:59 `e.mood || ''`). Detail/Home/PotDetail/AllExpenses all already conditional-render on truthy mood. Risk: existing data unchanged (still has 😌 stamps from before this change); only new entries differ. A future "rebuild" or backfill is unnecessary because the silent-default was never a meaningful signal · Impact: clean default semantics for any future analytics that wants to count "% of users who actively rate spends" — the metric becomes possible because NULL is now a distinguishable category.
- 2026-05-20 · 2.D.05 Scan review's hard-coded mood:'😌' flipped to null, but no mood UI added · User-selected option at Step 2 Q2 (Scan was in scope) but the cheapest correct interpretation is dropping the silent default without adding a new affordance. Scan.js has no mood picker today — adding one would be UX scope (where in the review screen does it go? collapsed-by-default or expanded? does the user even think about mood during a receipt scan?) that the task doesn't license. Setting `mood: null` aligns Scan with the new Add/Edit semantics: if you didn't actively rate this spend, it stays unrated. A future task can add a chip-style mood opt-in to the Scan review screen if user research shows it's wanted · Impact: every receipt-scanned expense now stores mood=null instead of the silent 😌 default; downstream readers and filters already handle that. Zero risk of regression because the Scan review screen had nothing to remove.
- 2026-05-20 · 2.D.05 EditExpense's `initialMoodIdx = e?.mood ? MOODS.indexOf(e.mood) : 1` line intentionally untouched · User-selected option at Step 2 Q4. The line determines which face is *visually* selected when the picker is in its expanded state — moodOn is the separate gate that decides whether that index ever reaches the DB. Touching the line would couple the two concerns (collapse-state and default-index-when-expanded) and risk subtly changing what happens when a user opens an unmoded row and then taps Add mood — they should see a sensible default (Worth it = 😌) preselected when the picker first appears, not 😍. Keeping the line as-is preserves that affordance · Impact: the `?? 1` fallback survives, but it's now scoped only to the "user has actively opted into the picker for this edit session" path. The silent-write side effect is gone because moodOn=false short-circuits the save · Touched files: only EditExpense.js's two save callsites and the render block — no logic mutation.
- 2026-05-19 · 5.14 chip "tap fills, second tap clears" with manual-edit cancelling applied flag · User-selected option at Step 2. The simplest "tap fills, second tap dismisses entirely" model would either hide the chip after the user accepts (so they can't get back to the predicted amount once they typed past it) or fight the user when they manually adjust ("did the chip get re-applied?"). The chosen model: tap = apply (chip flips coral/✓), tap again = clear (back to 0.00, chip flips back to cream/🕒), manual keypad press = clears the applied flag but leaves the typed value alone. So the user can: predict-and-accept (one tap), predict-then-tweak (tap, then keypad-edit — chip flips back to cream but stays visible so a re-tap can re-prefill), or ignore-the-chip-entirely (chip disappears as soon as a non-zero amount is typed without the chip ever being tapped) · Impact: chip is dismissible at any time without an explicit dismiss button (just type past it). The applied-coral state doubles as the "second tap clears" affordance — no extra UX to learn.

---

## Notes on resuming a session

When you re-engage with me (Claude) on this project after a break, do:

1. **Read `/docs/10-final/task_tracker.md`** (this file) first.
2. **Scan for `[/]` (in progress) and `[!]` (blocked)** lines — those tell you where I stopped mid-flow.
3. **Check the "Currently active" line** under the legend — it points to the active task.
4. **Read any new entries in the Completion log and Decision log** to understand what changed.
5. **Confirm with the user** which task to start or resume before touching code (Rule 1, Rule 15).

This file is the durable contract. The in-tool TaskList is session-scoped and is rebuilt per session from this file.
